2.14
and
Y\in\mathcal{Y}
, we directly have

\|b_{1}^{2}\big((1+\mu)^{-\frac{1}{4}}-1\big)\Lambda Y_{2}\|_{H^{1}}\lesssim|\mu zb_{1}^{2}|\lesssim\frac{1}{s^{3}\log s},

which implies that

10m_{0}b_{1}^{2}(1+\mu)^{-\frac{1}{4}}\Lambda Y_{2}=10m_{0}b_{1}^{2}\Gamma(\Lambda Y)+10m_{0}zb_{1}^{2}\partial_{y}Y_{2}+O_{\mathcal{S}}(1).

On the other hand, from the definition of
U_{1}
in (
2.26
), we decompose

b_{1}\Lambda U_{1}=b_{1}^{3}\Lambda\left((E_{1}+F_{2})\phi\right)+5(3m_{0}z+a_{2})b_{1}^{3}\Lambda Y_{2}.

Using again Definition
2.14
and
Y\in\mathcal{Y}
, we directly have

\|(3m_{0}z+a_{2})b_{1}^{3}\Lambda Y_{2}\|_{H^{1}}\lesssim z^{2}b_{1}^{3}\lesssim\frac{1}{s^{3}\log s},

which implies that

b_{1}\Lambda U_{1}=b_{1}^{3}\Lambda\left((E_{1}+F_{2})\phi\right)+O_{\mathcal{S}}(1).

Similarly, we also check that

\|b_{1}(2\alpha m_{0}z+a_{0})e^{-z}\Lambda Y_{2}\|_{H^{1}}\lesssim b_{1}z^{2}e^{-z}\lesssim\frac{1}{s^{3}\log s}.

We see that the estimate (
2.58
) follows directly from the above estimates.

Step 9.
Conclusion. Combining (
2.38
), (
2.39
), (
2.40
)–(
2.44
), (
2.54
) and (
2.58
) with an elementary computation, we complete the proof for Proposition
2.18
.
∎

Last, we establish some technical estimates related to the error terms
(\Psi_{i})_{i=1}^{4}
.

Lemma 2.19
.

The following estimates hold true.

(i)

Estimates related to
\Psi_{1}(V)
.

We have

\|\Psi_{1}(V)\|_{H^{1}}\lesssim\frac{|\dot{z}-b_{1}z-\mu|}{s\log s}+\frac{1}{s^{\frac{5}{2}}\log s}.

In particular, we have

\left|\left(\Psi_{1}(V),Q_{1}\right)\right|+\left|\left(\Psi_{1}(V),Q_{2}\right)\right|\lesssim\frac{|\dot{z}-b_{1}z-\mu|}{s^{2}\log s}+\frac{1}{s^{3}\log s}.

(ii)

Estimates related to
\Psi_{2}(V)
.

We have

\displaystyle\Psi_{2}(V)

\displaystyle=-\dot{z}e^{-z}\left(A_{1}+B_{2}\right)\phi+\frac{\dot{\mu}}{2}b_{1}^{2}\phi\Gamma(\Lambda F)

\displaystyle+O_{H^{1}}\left(\frac{|\dot{z}|}{s^{\frac{3}{2}}\log s}+\frac{|\dot{\mu}|}{s\log s}+\frac{|\dot{b}_{1}|}{s^{\frac{1}{2}}}+\frac{1}{s^{\frac{5}{2}}\log s}\right).

In particular, we have

\displaystyle\left|\left(\Psi_{2}(V),Q_{1}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{|\dot{z}|}{s^{2}\log s}+\frac{|\dot{\mu}|}{s^{2}\log s}+\frac{|\dot{b}_{1}|}{s}+\frac{1}{s^{3}\log s},

\displaystyle\left(\Psi_{2}(V),Q_{2}\right)

\displaystyle=-2\alpha m_{0}^{2}\dot{z}ze^{-z}+\frac{5}{2}m_{0}b_{1}\dot{\mu}\left(Y-2\Lambda Y,Q\right)

\displaystyle+O\left(\frac{|\dot{z}|}{s^{2}\log s}+\frac{|\dot{\mu}|}{s^{2}\log s}+\frac{|\dot{b}_{1}|}{s}+\frac{1}{s^{3}\log s}\right).

(iii)

Estimates related to
\Psi_{3}(V)
.

We have

\|\Psi_{3}(V)\|_{H^{1}}\lesssim\frac{1}{s^{\frac{5}{2}}\log s}.

In particular, we have

\displaystyle(\Psi_{3}(V),Q_{1})

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha}{4}m_{0}^{2}\zeta_{2}z^{2}e^{-z}+\frac{\alpha}{2}m_{0}^{2}\mu ze^{-z}+O\left(\frac{1}{s^{3}\log s}\right),

\displaystyle(\Psi_{3}(V),Q_{2})

\displaystyle=-\frac{\alpha}{4}m_{0}^{2}b_{1}z^{2}e^{-z}+O\left(\frac{1}{s^{3}\log s}\right).

(iv)

Estimates related to
\Psi_{4}(V)
.

We have

\displaystyle|\Psi_{4}(V)|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{|\dot{b}_{1}|}{s}\log s\mathbf{1}_{[\frac{s}{2},\frac{5s}{2}]}(y)+\frac{1}{s^{3}}\mathbf{1}_{[\frac{s}{2},\frac{5s}{2}]}(y),

\displaystyle|\partial_{y}\Psi_{4}(V)|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{|\dot{b}_{1}|}{s}\mathbf{1}_{[\frac{s}{2},\frac{5s}{2}]}(y)+\frac{1}{s^{3}\log s}\mathbf{1}_{[\frac{s}{2},\frac{5s}{2}]}(y).

In particular, we have

\left|\left(\Psi_{4}(V),Q_{1}\right)\right|+\left|\left(\Psi_{4}(V),Q_{2}\right)\right|\lesssim\frac{|\dot{b}_{1}|}{s}+\frac{1}{s^{3}\log s}.

Proof.

Proof of (i). The proof for the
H^{1}
norm estimate of
\Psi_{1}(V)
is a direct consequence of Definition
2.14
and Lemma
2.16
. On the other hand, using the exponential decay of
Q
, we find

|(\partial_{y}X_{2},Q_{1})|+|(\partial_{y}Y_{2},Q_{1})|\lesssim z^{5}e^{-z}\lesssim s^{-1}.

In addition, from Remark
2.2
and Remark
2.5
, we check that

(\partial_{y}X_{2},Q_{2})=(\partial_{y}Y_{2},Q_{2})=0\quad\mbox{and}\quad\left(\Lambda X_{2},Q_{2}\right)=\frac{1}{2}(X,Q)+(yX^{\prime},Q).

Combining the above estimates with Definition
2.14
, we complete the proof for (i).

Proof of (ii). The proof for the
H^{1}
norm estimate of
\Psi_{2}(V)
is also a direct consequence of Definition
2.14
and Lemma
2.16
. On the other hand, we rewrite

\displaystyle A_{1}+B_{2}

\displaystyle=(A_{1}-2\alpha m_{0}y)+2\alpha m_{0}y\big(1-(1+\mu)^{\frac{3}{4}}\big)

\displaystyle+(B_{2}+2\alpha m_{0}(1+\mu)^{\frac{3}{4}}(y-z))+2\alpha m_{0}(1+\mu)^{\frac{3}{4}}z,

which implies that
6
6

6

Here, we use the fact that
m_{0}=\frac{1}{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}}Q(y){\rm d}y
.

\displaystyle\left(-\dot{z}e^{-z}\left(A_{1}+B_{2}\right)\phi,Q_{1}\right)

\displaystyle=O\left(\frac{|\dot{z}|}{s^{2}\log s}\right),

\displaystyle\left(-\dot{z}e^{-z}\left(A_{1}+B_{2}\right)\phi,Q_{2}\right)

\displaystyle=-8m_{0}^{2}\dot{z}ze^{-z}+O\left(\frac{|{\dot{z}}|}{s^{2}\log s}\right).

Then, from Remark
2.2
, we compute

\displaystyle\left(-10\alpha m_{0}\dot{z}ze^{-z}(Y_{2}+\partial_{y}Y_{2}),Q_{1}\right)

\displaystyle=O\left(\frac{|{\dot{z}}|}{s^{2}\log s}\right),

\displaystyle\left(-10\alpha m_{0}\dot{z}ze^{-z}(Y_{2}+\partial_{y}Y_{2}),Q_{2}\right)

\displaystyle=6\alpha m_{0}^{2}\dot{z}ze^{-z}+O\left(\frac{|{\dot{z}}|}{s^{2}\log s}\right).

Combining above estimates with Definition
2.14
, we complete the proof for (ii).

Proof of (iii). The proof for the
H^{1}
norm estimate of
\Psi_{3}(V)
is also a direct consequence of Definition
2.14
and Lemma
2.16
. On the other hand, using again Lemma
2.9
and Definition
2.14
, we compute
7
7

7

Here, we use the fact that
\alpha=\frac{c_{Q}}{m_{0}^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{y}Q^{5}(y){\rm d}y
.

\displaystyle\frac{5c_{Q}}{4}b_{1}z^{2}e^{-z}\left(\partial_{y}\left(e^{-(y-z)}Q^{4}(y-z)\right),Q_{1}\right)

\displaystyle=O\left(\frac{1}{s^{3}\log s}\right),

\displaystyle\frac{5c_{Q}}{4}b_{1}z^{2}e^{-z}\left(\partial_{y}\left(e^{-(y-z)}Q^{4}(y-z)\right),Q_{2}\right)

\displaystyle=-\frac{\alpha}{4}m_{0}^{2}b_{1}z^{2}e^{-z}+O\left(\frac{1}{s^{3}\log s}\right).

Similar as above, we also compute

\displaystyle\frac{5c_{Q}}{4}\zeta_{2}z^{2}e^{-z}\left(\partial_{y}(e^{y}Q^{4}(y)),Q_{1}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha}{4}m_{0}^{2}\zeta_{2}z^{2}e^{-z},

\displaystyle\frac{5c_{Q}}{4}\zeta_{2}z^{2}e^{-z}\left(\partial_{y}(e^{y}Q^{4}(y)),Q_{2}\right)

\displaystyle=O\left(\frac{1}{s^{3}\log s}\right),

\displaystyle\frac{5c_{Q}}{2}\mu ze^{-z}\left(\partial_{y}\left(e^{y}Q^{4}(y)\right),Q_{1}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha}{2}m_{0}^{2}\mu ze^{-z},

\displaystyle\frac{5c_{Q}}{2}\mu ze^{-z}\left(\partial_{y}\left(e^{y}Q^{4}(y)\right),Q_{2}\right)

\displaystyle=O\left(\frac{1}{s^{3}\log s}\right).

Then, from Lemma
2.9
, Remark
2.2
and Lemma
2.8
,

(\partial_{y}F_{2},Q_{2})=(\partial_{y}Y_{2},Q_{2})=(\partial_{y}(Q_{2}^{4}),Q_{2})=0,

|(\partial_{y}F_{2},Q_{1})|+|(\partial_{y}Y_{2},Q_{1})|+|(\partial_{y}(Q_{2}^{4}),Q_{1})|\lesssim s^{-1}.

Combining above estimates with Definition
2.14
, we complete the proof for (iii).

Proof of (iv).
From Definition
2.14
and the definition of
\phi
in (
2.25
), we check that

\displaystyle\left|\partial_{s}\phi\right|\lesssim\bigg|\bigg(\frac{\dot{a}}{a}\bigg)\chi^{\prime}(ay)\bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim(s\log s)|\dot{b}_{1}|\textbf{1}_{[\frac{s}{2},\frac{5s}{2}]}(y),

\displaystyle\left|\partial_{y}\partial_{s}\phi\right|\lesssim\bigg|\bigg(\frac{\dot{a}}{a}\bigg)\chi^{\prime}(ay)\bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim(\log s)|\dot{b}_{1}|\textbf{1}_{[\frac{s}{2},\frac{5s}{2}]}(y).

Similar as above, we also check that

\displaystyle\left|\partial_{y}\phi\right|\lesssim s^{-1}\textbf{1}_{[\frac{s}{2},\frac{5s}{2}]}(y)\quad\mbox{and}\quad\left|\partial^{2}_{y}\phi\right|\lesssim s^{-2}\textbf{1}_{[\frac{s}{2},\frac{5s}{2}]}(y).

Recall that, we rewrite the term
R
by

\displaystyle R

\displaystyle=e^{-z}\big(B_{2}+2\alpha m_{0}(1+\mu)^{\frac{3}{4}}(y-z)\big)+2\alpha m_{0}(1+\mu)^{\frac{3}{4}}ze^{-z}

\displaystyle+2b_{1}\big((1+\mu)^{\frac{1}{4}}-1\big)m_{0}-b_{1}(X_{2}+2(1+\mu)^{\frac{1}{4}}m_{0})+(b_{1}-\zeta_{2})X_{2}

\displaystyle+b_{1}(X_{1}+2m_{0})+e^{-z}(A_{1}-2\alpha m_{0}y)+2\alpha m_{0}e^{-z}y\big(1-(1+\mu)^{\frac{3}{4}}\big),

which implies that

|R|\textbf{1}_{[\frac{s}{2},\frac{5s}{2}]}(y)\lesssim s^{-2}\textbf{1}_{[\frac{s}{2},\frac{5s}{2}]}(y)\ \ \mbox{and}\ \ |\partial_{y}R|\textbf{1}_{[\frac{s}{2},\frac{5s}{2}]}(y)\lesssim s^{-2}\log^{-1}s\textbf{1}_{[\frac{s}{2},\frac{5s}{2}]}(y).

In addition, we rewrite the term
E_{1}+F_{2}
by

\displaystyle E_{1}+F_{2}

\displaystyle=(E_{1}-3m_{0}y)+3m_{0}y\big(1-(1+\mu)^{\frac{3}{4}}\big)

\displaystyle+(F_{2}+3m_{0}(1+\mu)^{\frac{3}{4}}(y-z))+3m_{0}(1+\mu)^{\frac{3}{4}}z,

which implies that

\displaystyle|E_{1}+F_{2}|\textbf{1}_{[\frac{s}{2},\frac{5s}{2}]}(y)

\displaystyle\lesssim(1+z+|\mu y|)\textbf{1}_{[\frac{s}{2},\frac{5s}{2}]}(y)\lesssim(\log s)\textbf{1}_{[\frac{s}{2},\frac{5s}{2}]}(y),

\displaystyle|\partial_{y}E_{1}+\partial_{y}F_{2}|\textbf{1}_{[\frac{s}{2},\frac{5s}{2}]}(y)

\displaystyle\lesssim(1+|\mu y|)\textbf{1}_{[\frac{s}{2},\frac{5s}{2}]}(y)\lesssim\textbf{1}_{[\frac{s}{2},\frac{5s}{2}]}(y).

It follows from Definition
2.14
and the definition of
\Psi_{4}(V)
in Proposition
2.18
that

\displaystyle\left|\Psi_{4}(V)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\frac{|E_{1}+F_{2}|}{s^{2}\log^{2}s}\right)|\partial_{s}\phi|+\left(\frac{|yR|}{s\log s}\right)|\partial_{y}\phi|

\displaystyle+\left(\frac{|E_{1}+F_{2}|}{s^{2}\log^{2}s}\right)|\partial_{y}\phi|+|R|(\partial_{s}\phi|+\partial_{y}\phi|)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{|\dot{b}_{1}|}{s}\log s\textbf{1}_{[\frac{s}{2},\frac{5s}{2}]}(y)+\frac{1}{s^{3}\log s}\textbf{1}_{[\frac{s}{2},\frac{5s}{2}]}(y)+\frac{1}{s^{3}}\textbf{1}_{[\frac{s}{2},\frac{5s}{2}]}(y).

Similarly, we also have

\displaystyle\left|\partial_{y}\Psi_{4}(V)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\frac{|E_{1}+F_{2}|}{s^{2}\log^{2}s}+|R|\right)|\partial_{s}\partial_{y}\phi|+\left(\frac{|yR|}{s\log s}+|R|\right)|\partial^{2}_{y}\phi|

\displaystyle+\left(\frac{|\partial_{y}E_{1}+\partial_{y}F_{2}|}{s^{2}\log^{2}s}+|\partial_{y}R|\right)|\partial_{s}\phi|+\left(\frac{|R|+|y\partial_{y}R|}{s\log s}\right)|\partial_{y}\phi|

\displaystyle+\left(\frac{|E_{1}+F_{2}|}{s^{2}\log^{2}s}\right)|\partial^{2}_{y}\phi|+\left(\frac{|\partial_{y}(E_{1}+F_{2})|}{s^{2}\log^{2}s}\right)|\partial_{y}\phi|+|\partial_{y}R||\partial_{y}\phi|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{|\dot{b}_{1}|}{s}\textbf{1}_{[\frac{s}{2},\frac{5s}{2}]}(y)+\frac{1}{s^{3}\log^{2}s}\textbf{1}_{[\frac{s}{2},\frac{5s}{2}]}(y)+\frac{1}{s^{3}\log s}\textbf{1}_{[\frac{s}{2},\frac{5s}{2}]}(y).

Based on the above estimates and the exponential decay of
Q
, we obtain

\displaystyle|(\Psi_{4}(V),Q_{1})|+|(\Psi_{4}(V),Q_{2})|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{|\dot{b}_{1}|}{s}\log s\big\|Q_{1}\textbf{1}_{[\frac{s}{2},\frac{5s}{2}]}(y)\big\|_{L^{1}}+\frac{|\dot{b}_{1}|}{s}\log s\big\|Q_{2}\textbf{1}_{[\frac{s}{2},\frac{5s}{2}]}(y)\big\|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{s^{3}}\big\|Q_{1}\textbf{1}_{[\frac{s}{2},\frac{5s}{2}]}(y)\big\|_{L^{1}}+\frac{1}{s^{3}}\big\|Q_{2}\textbf{1}_{[\frac{s}{2},\frac{5s}{2}]}(y)\big\|_{L^{1}}\lesssim\frac{|\dot{b}_{1}|}{s}+\frac{1}{s^{3}\log s}.

Combining the above estimates, we complete the proof for (iv).
∎

Lemma 2.20
.

The following estimates hold true.

(i)

Estimate related to
\Psi_{1}(V)
. We have

\displaystyle\left|(\Psi_{1}(V),\Lambda Q)\right|+\left|(\Psi_{1}(V),\Gamma\Lambda Q)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{|\dot{z}-b_{1}z-\mu|}{s\log s}+\frac{1}{s^{3}},

\displaystyle\left|(\Psi_{1}(V),\partial_{y}Q_{1})\right|+\left|(\Psi_{1}(V),\partial_{y}Q_{2})\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{|\dot{z}-b_{1}z-\mu|}{s\log s}+\frac{1}{s^{3}}.

(ii)

Estimate related to
\Psi_{2}(V)
. We have

\displaystyle\left|(\Psi_{2}(V),\Lambda Q)\right|+\left|(\Psi_{2}(V),\Gamma\Lambda Q)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{|\dot{z}|}{s^{2}}+\frac{|\dot{\mu}|}{s\log s}+\frac{|\dot{b}_{1}|}{s}+\frac{1}{s^{3}},

\displaystyle\left|(\Psi_{2}(V),\partial_{y}Q_{1})\right|+\left|(\Psi_{2}(V),\partial_{y}Q_{2})\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{|\dot{z}|}{s^{2}}+\frac{|\dot{\mu}|}{s\log s}+\frac{|\dot{b}_{1}|}{s}+\frac{1}{s^{3}}.

(iii)

Estimate related to
\Psi_{3}(V)
. We have

\displaystyle\left|(\Psi_{3}(V),\Lambda Q)\right|+\left|(\Psi_{3}(V),\Gamma\Lambda Q)\right|\lesssim\frac{1}{s^{3}},

\displaystyle\left|(\Psi_{3}(V),\partial_{y}Q_{1})\right|+\left|(\Psi_{3}(V),\partial_{y}Q_{2})\right|\lesssim\frac{1}{s^{3}}.

(iv)

Estimate related to
\Psi_{4}(V)
. We have

\displaystyle\left|(\Psi_{4}(V),\Lambda Q)\right|+\left|(\Psi_{4}(V),\Gamma\Lambda Q)\right|\lesssim\frac{|\dot{b}_{1}|}{s}+\frac{1}{s^{3}},

\displaystyle\left|(\Psi_{4}(V),\partial_{y}Q_{1})\right|+\left|(\Psi_{4}(V),\partial_{y}Q_{2})\right|\lesssim\frac{|\dot{b}_{1}|}{s}+\frac{1}{s^{3}}.

Proof.

The proof is based on an argument similar to the proof for Lemma
2.19
and the definition of
(\Psi_{i})_{i=1}^{4}
in Proposition
2.18
, and we omit it.
∎

3.
Dynamics close to the two-bubble solution

3.1.
Modulation of the approximate solution

In this subsection, we state a standard modulation result around the approximate two-bubble solution
V
. The proof of such result relies on a standard argument based on the Implicit Function Theorem (see
e.g.

[
33
, Lemma 2.5]
). For the sake of completeness and the reader’s convenience, we provide the details for the proof here.

Proposition 3.1
.

Let
u(t)
be a solution of (
1.1
) on a time interval
[t_{0},t_{1}]
. Suppose that at some time
\bar{t}\in[t_{0},t_{1}]
, the solution
u(\bar{t})
admits a geometric decomposition in the following sense: there exist some geometric parameters
\bar{\mathcal{G}}=(\bar{\lambda},\bar{z},\bar{\mu},\bar{x}_{1},\bar{b}_{1},\bar{b}_{2})\in(0,\infty)^{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{4}
such that, defining

\bar{\varepsilon}(y)=\bar{\lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}u\big(\bar{t},\bar{\lambda}y+\bar{x}_{1}\big)-V\big(y;(\bar{z},\bar{\mu},\bar{b}_{1},\bar{b}_{2})\big)\in H^{1},

the function
\bar{\varepsilon}
satisfies the following orthogonality conditions

\displaystyle(\bar{\varepsilon},\Lambda Q)

\displaystyle=(\bar{\varepsilon},Q^{\prime})=(\bar{\varepsilon},Q)=0,

(3.1)

\displaystyle(\bar{\varepsilon},\overline{\Gamma}\Lambda Q)

\displaystyle=(\bar{\varepsilon},\overline{\Gamma}Q^{\prime})=(\bar{\varepsilon},\overline{\Gamma}Q)=0.

Here, we denote

\displaystyle(\overline{\Gamma}Q)(y)

\displaystyle=(1+\bar{\mu})^{\frac{1}{4}}\big[Q((1+\bar{\mu})^{\frac{1}{2}}(y-\bar{z}))\big],

\displaystyle(\overline{\Gamma}Q^{\prime})(y)

\displaystyle=(1+\bar{\mu})^{\frac{1}{4}}\big[Q^{\prime}((1+\bar{\mu})^{\frac{1}{2}}(y-\bar{z}))\big],

\displaystyle(\overline{\Gamma}\Lambda Q)(y)

\displaystyle=(1+\bar{\mu})^{\frac{1}{4}}\big[(\Lambda Q)((1+\bar{\mu})^{\frac{1}{2}}(y-\bar{z}))\big].

We assume moreover that there exists a small enough constant
\delta>0
such that

(\bar{\lambda},\bar{z})\in(0,\infty)\times(\delta^{-1},\infty)\quad\mbox{and}\quad\|\bar{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{1}}+|\bar{b}_{1}|+|\bar{b}_{2}|+|\bar{\mu}|<\bar{z}^{-3}<\delta^{2}.

(3.2)

Then there exist a time
\tau>0
small enough and a unique
C^{1}
geometric function
\mathcal{G}(t)=(\lambda(t),z(t),\mu(t),x_{1}(t),b_{1}(t),b_{2}(t))\in(0,\infty)^{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{4}
on
[\bar{t}-\tau,\bar{t}+\tau]
such that, for all
t\in[\bar{t}-\tau,\bar{t}+\tau]
,
\varepsilon(t)
being defined by

\varepsilon(t,y)=\lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}(t)u\left(t,\lambda(t)y+x_{1}(t)\right)-V\left(y;(z,\mu,b_{1},b_{2})\right),

(3.3)

it satisfies the orthogonality conditions

\displaystyle(\varepsilon(t),\Lambda Q)

\displaystyle=(\varepsilon(t),\partial_{y}Q_{1})=(\varepsilon(t),Q_{1})=0,

(3.4)

\displaystyle(\varepsilon(t),\Gamma\Lambda Q)

\displaystyle=(\varepsilon(t),\partial_{y}Q_{2})=(\varepsilon(t),Q_{2})=0.

In addition, we have
\mathcal{G}(\bar{t})=\bar{\mathcal{G}}
and
(\varepsilon(t),\mathcal{G}(t))
satisfies

\|\varepsilon\|_{H^{1}}+|b_{1}|+|b_{2}|+|\mu|+|z|^{-1}+|x_{1}-\bar{x}_{1}|+\left|\frac{\lambda}{\bar{\lambda}}-1\right|\lesssim\varrho(\delta).

Proof.

Using the scaling and translation invariances of (
1.1
), we can assume that
(\bar{\lambda},\bar{x}_{1})=(1,0)
. From now on, we denote
(\bar{u},\overline{\mathcal{G}})=(u(\bar{t}),1,\bar{z},\bar{\mu},0,\bar{b}_{1},\bar{b}_{2})
.

Fix
t\in[t_{0},t_{1}]
. We consider the map

\displaystyle\Phi:(u,\mathcal{G})\mapsto(\Phi_{1},\Phi_{2},\Phi_{3},\Phi_{4},\Phi_{5},\Phi_{6})\in\mathbb{R}^{6}.

Here, we denote

\displaystyle\Phi_{1}

\displaystyle=(\varepsilon(t),\Lambda Q),\quad\ \Phi_{2}=(\varepsilon(t),\partial_{y}Q_{2}),\ \ \Phi_{3}=(\varepsilon(t),\Gamma\Lambda Q),

\displaystyle\Phi_{4}

\displaystyle=(\varepsilon(t),\partial_{y}Q_{1}),\ \ \Phi_{5}=(\varepsilon(t),Q_{1}),\quad\ \ \Phi_{6}=(\varepsilon(t),Q_{2}).

Note that, the set of conditions in (
3.4
) is equivalent to
\Psi(u,\mathcal{G})=0
. In what follows, we solve this nonlinear system around
(\bar{u},\overline{\mathcal{G}})
by the Implicit Function Theorem.

First, it is easy to check that

\Psi(\bar{u},\overline{\mathcal{G}})=0
. Second, by (
3.2
) and (
3.3
), we find

\displaystyle\left.\frac{\partial\varepsilon}{\partial x_{1}}\right|_{(u,\mathcal{G})=(\bar{u},\overline{\mathcal{G}})}

\displaystyle=Q^{\prime}-Q^{\prime}(\cdot-\bar{z})+O_{H^{1}_{loc}}\left(\bar{z}^{-2}\right),

\displaystyle\left.\frac{\partial\varepsilon}{\partial b_{1}}\right|_{(u,\mathcal{G})=(\bar{u},\overline{\mathcal{G}})}

\displaystyle=10m_{0}Y(\cdot-\bar{z})-X+O_{H^{1}_{loc}}\left(\bar{z}^{-2}\right),

\displaystyle\left.\frac{\partial\varepsilon}{\partial\lambda}\right|_{(u,\mathcal{G})=(\bar{u},\overline{\mathcal{G}})}

\displaystyle=\Lambda Q-(\Lambda Q)(\cdot-\bar{z})-\bar{z}Q^{\prime}(\cdot-\bar{z})+O_{H^{1}_{loc}}\left(\bar{z}^{-2}\right).

Using again (
3.2
) and (
3.3
), we also find

\displaystyle\left.\frac{\partial\varepsilon}{\partial b_{2}}\right|_{(u,\mathcal{G})=(\bar{u},\overline{\mathcal{G}})}

\displaystyle=X(\cdot-\bar{z})+O_{H^{1}_{loc}}\left(\bar{z}^{-2}\right),

\displaystyle\left.\frac{\partial\varepsilon}{\partial z}\right|_{(u,\mathcal{G})=(\bar{u},\overline{\mathcal{G}})}

\displaystyle=-Q^{\prime}(\cdot-\bar{z})+O_{H^{1}_{loc}}\left(\bar{z}^{-2}\right),

\displaystyle\left.\frac{\partial\varepsilon}{\partial\mu}\right|_{(u,\mathcal{G})=(\bar{u},\overline{\mathcal{G}})}

\displaystyle=(\Lambda Q)(\cdot-\bar{z})+O_{H^{1}_{loc}}\left(\bar{z}^{-2}\right).

Therefore, using integration by parts, for any
(u,\mathcal{G})
around
(\bar{u},\overline{\mathcal{G}})
, we find

D_{\mathcal{G}}\Phi=\begin{pmatrix}\|\Lambda Q\|_{L^{2}}^{2}&0&0&0&O(1)&0\\
-\bar{z}\|Q^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}&-\|Q^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}&0&-\|Q^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}&0&0\\
-\|\Lambda Q\|_{L^{2}}^{2}&0&\|\Lambda Q\|^{2}_{L^{2}}&0&O(1)&O(1)\\
0&0&0&\|Q^{\prime}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}&0&0\\
0&0&0&0&m_{0}^{2}&0\\
0&0&0&0&2m_{0}^{2}&-m_{0}^{2}\end{pmatrix}+O\left(\bar{z}^{-2}\right).

Here, we use the fact that
8
8

8

See Proposition
2.1
, Remark
2.2
and Remark
2.5
.

\displaystyle(\Lambda Q,Q)=(\Lambda Q,Q^{\prime})=(X,Q)+m_{0}^{2}

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle(Q,Q^{\prime})=(X,Q^{\prime})=(Y,Q^{\prime})=(Y,Q)+\frac{3}{5}m_{0}^{2}

\displaystyle=0.

Thus, from the smallness condition (
3.2
),
D_{\mathcal{G}}\Phi(u,\mathcal{G})
is an invertible matrix for
\delta>0
small enough, with a lower bound uniform around
(\bar{u},\overline{\mathcal{G}})
.
Therefore, by the uniform variant of the Implicit
Function Theorem, there exist
\delta_{1}\in(0,\infty)
small enough (independent with
\bar{z}
and
\delta
) and a
C^{1}
map

\Pi:B_{H^{1}}(\bar{u},\delta_{1}\bar{z}^{-3})\to B_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}(\overline{\mathcal{G}},\bar{z}^{-3}),

such that for all
(u,\mathcal{G})\in B_{H^{1}}(\bar{u},\delta_{1}\bar{z}^{-3})\times B_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}(\overline{\mathcal{G}},\bar{z}^{-3})
,

\Phi(u,\mathcal{G})=0\ \ \mbox{if and only if}\ \ \mathcal{G}=\Pi(u).

Moreover, taking
\delta>0
small enough, for any
\bar{\varepsilon}
satisfying (
3.2
), we have

\|\varepsilon\|_{H^{1}}+|\mathcal{G}-\overline{\mathcal{G}}|\lesssim\|\bar{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{1}}+|\mathcal{G}-\overline{\mathcal{G}}|\lesssim\|\overline{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{1}}+|\Pi(u)-\Pi(\bar{u})|\lesssim\delta,

which directly completes the proof for Proposition
3.1
.
∎

3.2.
Backwards uniform estimates

In this subsection, we state the uniform estimates for particular backwards solutions. As usual in the construction of solutions with explicit asymptotic behavior, the key point is to carefully adjust their final data to obtain the estimates that are uniform in the special regime of Theorem
1.1
.

Let
\mathcal{G}^{in}=(\lambda^{in},z^{in},\mu^{in},x_{1}^{in},b_{1}^{in},b_{2}^{in})\in(0,\infty)^{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{4}
to be chosen with
0<\lambda^{in}\ll 1
,
1\ll z^{in}<\infty
and
|\mu^{in}|+|x_{1}^{in}|+|b_{1}^{in}|+|b_{2}^{in}|\ll 1
. Let
u(t)
for
t\in[T^{*},T_{n}]
be the solution of (
1.1
) with the following finial data

u(T_{n},x)=\frac{1}{(\lambda^{in})^{\frac{1}{2}}}V^{in}\left(\frac{x-x_{1}^{in}}{\lambda^{in}}\right)\in H^{1}.

(3.5)

Here, we denote

V^{in}(y)=V(y;(z^{in},\mu^{in},b_{1}^{in},b_{2}^{in}))\in H^{1}.

Note that, the finial data
u(T_{n})
satisfies (
3.1
)–(
3.2
), and thus, by Proposition
3.1
and continuity of the solution flow for (
1.1
) in
H^{1}
, it exists and admits decomposition which satisfies (
3.2
)–(
3.4
) on some time interval
[T^{*},T_{n}]
. In addition, we denote
(\varepsilon,\mathcal{G})
by the error term and geometric parameters related to the decomposition in Proposition
3.1
.

For
s^{in}\gg 1
, we normalize the rescaled time
s
as follows: for
t\in[T^{*},T_{n}]
, we set

s(t)=s^{in}-\int_{t}^{T_{n}}\frac{{\rm d}\sigma}{\lambda^{3}(\sigma)}\in(-\infty,s^{in}].

(3.6)

Observe from Proposition
3.1
that

(\varepsilon(s^{in}),\mathcal{G}(s^{in}))=(0,\lambda^{in},z^{in},\mu^{in},x_{1}^{in},b_{1}^{in},b_{2}^{in}).

(3.7)

From now on, we fix
T_{n}=n
and choose
s^{in}
such that

\left|T_{n}-\frac{s^{in}}{\log^{\frac{1}{2}}s^{in}}\right|\leq\frac{s^{in}}{\log^{\frac{3}{4}}s^{in}}.

(3.8)

The following Proposition is the main part of the proof of Theorem
1.1
.

Proposition 3.2

(Uniform estimates)
.

There exists
s_{0}>1
such that for all
s^{in}>s_{0}
satisfying (
3.8
), there exist some choice of geometric parameters
\mathcal{G}^{in}=(\lambda^{in},z^{in},\mu^{in},x_{1}^{in},b_{1}^{in},b_{2}^{in})\in(0,\infty)^{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{4}
with

\displaystyle\lambda^{in}=\log^{-\frac{1}{6}}(s^{in}),\quad b_{2}^{in}=b_{1}^{in}-\frac{5\alpha}{2}z^{in}e^{-z^{in}},

(3.9)

\displaystyle\mu^{in}=5b^{in}z^{in},\quad b_{1}^{in}=\left(\frac{\alpha}{3}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(z^{in}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}e^{-\frac{1}{2}z^{in}},

\displaystyle\left|x_{1}^{in}-\frac{s^{in}}{\log^{\frac{1}{6}}s^{in}}\right|^{2}+\left|\left(\frac{1}{3\alpha}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}(z^{in})^{-\frac{1}{2}}e^{\frac{1}{2}z^{in}}-s^{in}\right|^{2}\leq\frac{(s^{in})^{2}}{\log s^{in}}.

such that the solution
u
of (
1.1
) corresponding to (
3.5
) exists and satisfies (
3.1
)–(
3.4
) on the rescaled time interval
[s_{0},s^{in}]
. Here, the rescaled time
s
is defined in (
3.6
). Moreover, the decomposition of
u
given by Proposition
3.1
on
[s_{0},s^{in}]

\varepsilon(s,y)=\lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}(s)u(s,\lambda(s)y+x_{1}(s))-V(y;(z,\mu,b_{1},b_{2})),

satisfies the following uniform estimates: for all
s\in[s_{0},s^{in}]
, we have

\displaystyle|z(s)-2\log s|\lesssim\log\log s,\quad|b_{1}(s)|\lesssim\frac{1}{s\log s},

(3.10)

\displaystyle\Big|x_{1}(s)-\frac{s}{\log^{\frac{1}{6}}s}\Big|\lesssim\frac{s}{\log^{\frac{1}{2}}s},\quad\|\varepsilon(s)\|_{H^{1}}\lesssim\frac{1}{s},

\displaystyle\Big|\lambda(s)-\frac{1}{\log^{\frac{1}{6}}s}\Big|\lesssim\frac{1}{\log^{\frac{2}{3}}s},\quad|\mu(s)|\lesssim\frac{1}{s},\quad|b_{2}(s)|\lesssim\frac{1}{s\log s}.

The key point in Proposition
3.2
is that
s_{0}>1
and the implied constants in (
3.10
) are independent of
s^{in}
as
s^{in}\to\infty
. The rest of the section is organized as follows. First, in Section
3.3
, we introduce the bootstrap assumption which related to the above uniform estimates. Then, in Section
3.4
–
3.5
, we devote to the proof of Proposition
3.2
based on a standard energy estimate. More precisely, we control the remainder term
\varepsilon
via energy estimate in the framework of the two-bubble solutions and estimate the geometric parameters
\mathcal{G}
via some ODE arguments.

3.3.
Bootstrap bounds

For
t\leq T_{n}
(
i.e.

s\leq s^{in}
), as long as
u(t)
is well-defined in
H^{1}
and satisfies (
3.1
)–(
3.2
), we decompose
u(t)
as in Proposition
3.1
. In particular, we denote by
(\varepsilon(s),\mathcal{G}(s))
the remainder term and the geometric parameters of the decomposition for
u(t)
. Let
\chi_{1}:\mathbb{R}\to[0,1]
be decreasing
C^{\infty}
function such that

\chi_{1}(y)=\left\{\begin{aligned} &1-e^{y}\ \ \mbox{on}\ (-\infty,-1],\\
&e^{-y}\quad\ \ \mbox{on}\ [2,\infty),\end{aligned}\right.\quad\mbox{and}\quad\chi^{\prime}_{1}<0\ \ \mbox{on}\ \mathbb{R}.

(3.11)

For
B>1
large to be chosen later, we define

\mathcal{N}_{B}(\varepsilon)=\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left((\partial_{y}\varepsilon)^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\phi_{1}\right){\rm d}y\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\quad\mbox{with}\ \ \phi_{1}(y)=\chi_{1}\left(\frac{y}{B}-s^{\frac{1}{2}}\right).

(3.12)

The proof of Proposition
3.2
is based on the following bootstrap estimates: for
C_{0}>1
to be chosen,

\left\{\begin{aligned} \frac{1}{2\log^{\frac{1}{6}}s}\leq\lambda(s)\leq\frac{3}{2\log^{\frac{1}{6}}s},\quad\mathcal{N}_{B}(\varepsilon)\leq\frac{C_{0}}{s^{\frac{3}{2}}\log s},\\
|\mu(s)|\leq\frac{10}{s},\quad\frac{1}{7s\log s}\leq b_{1}(s)\leq\frac{1}{5s\log s},\quad|\Theta(s)|\leq\frac{10}{s^{2}},\\
\Big|x_{1}(s)-\frac{s}{\log^{\frac{1}{6}}s}\Big|^{2}+\left|\left(\frac{1}{3\alpha}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}z^{-\frac{1}{2}}(s)e^{\frac{z(s)}{2}}-s\right|^{2}\leq\frac{s^{2}}{\log s}.\end{aligned}\right.

(3.13)

Note that, the estimate on
z
in (
3.13
) immediately implies that, for
s
large,

0<e^{-z(s)}\lesssim s^{-2}\log^{-1}s\quad\mbox{and}\quad|z(s)-2\log s|\lesssim\log\log s.

(3.14)

For
s_{0}>1
to be chosen large enough (independent of
s^{in}
), and all
s^{in}\gg s_{0}
, we set

s^{*}=\inf\left\{s\in[s_{0},s^{in}]:u(s)\ \mbox{satisfies}\ \eqref{equ:orthmodu1}-\eqref{equ:modu2}\ \mbox{and}\ \eqref{est:Boot}\ \mbox{on}\ [s,s^{in}]\right\}.

(3.15)

Observe that the choice of final data in (
3.5
) and the continuity of the flow for (
1.1
) directly imply
s^{*}<s^{in}<\infty
. Moreover, for any
s^{*}<s<s^{in}<\infty
, the bootstrap assumptions (
3.13
)–(
3.14
) guarantee that (
3.1
)–(
3.2
) hold on
[s,s^{in}]
whenever (
3.4
) and (
3.13
) hold on this interval. Therefore, to close the bootstrap argument concerning
s^{*}
, it suffices to justify the estimates in (
3.13
) rigorously.

We further observe that, for
s_{0}>1
large enough, the geometric parameters
\mathcal{G}(s)
satisfying (
3.13
) are admissible in the sense of Definition
2.14
. It follows that Proposition
2.18
remains valid under the bootstrap assumption (
3.13
).

In the rest of the section, the implied constant in
\lesssim
and
O
do not depend on the constant
C_{0}
appearing in the bootstrap assumption (
3.13
).

We first establish the
L^{2}
and
L^{\infty}
estimates of the remainder term
\varepsilon
.

Lemma 3.3
.

We have

\|\varepsilon(s)\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim s^{-1}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\|\varepsilon(s)\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim s^{-\frac{5}{4}}.

Proof.

First, from the orthogonality condition (
3.4
), we compute

\|u(s)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}=\|\varepsilon(s)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|V(s)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+2(\varepsilon,R\phi)+2(\varepsilon,U).

Note that, using the bootstrap assumption (
3.13
) and Lemma
2.9
,

\|V(s)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}=2\|Q\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+2(S,R\phi+U)+\|R\phi+U\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+O\left(s^{-2}\right).

In addition, using again the bootstrap assumption (
3.13
), we check that

(S,R\phi+U)=(S,b_{1}X_{1}-\zeta_{2}X_{2}-10m_{0}b_{1}Y_{2})+O\left(s^{-2}\right).

From Remark
2.2
and Remark
2.5
, we have

\displaystyle(S,b_{1}X_{1}-\zeta_{2}X_{2}-10m_{0}b_{1}Y_{2})

\displaystyle=b_{1}(Q_{1},X_{1}-X_{2}-10m_{0}Y_{2})+\left(1-(1+\mu)^{-\frac{3}{2}}\right)b_{1}(S,X_{2})

\displaystyle-b_{1}(Q_{2},X_{1}-X_{2}-10m_{0}Y_{2})-\Theta(1+\mu)^{-\frac{3}{2}}(S,X_{2})=O\left(s^{-2}\right).

Here, we use the fact that

\displaystyle(Q_{1},X_{1}-X_{2}-10m_{0}Y_{2})

\displaystyle=-m_{0}^{2}+O\left(s^{-2}\right),

\displaystyle(Q_{2},X_{1}-X_{2}-10m_{0}Y_{2})

\displaystyle=-m_{0}^{2}+O\left(s^{-2}\right).

Then, using Lemma
2.16
and the bootstrap assumption (
3.13
),

\displaystyle\|R\phi\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|U\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(b_{1}^{4}+e^{-2z}\right)\|(A_{1}+B_{2})\phi\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+b_{1}^{2}\|Y_{2}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\left(b_{1}^{4}+e^{-2z}\right)\|(E_{1}+F_{2})\phi\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+z^{2}e^{-2z}\|Y_{2}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle+b_{1}^{2}\|(X_{1}-X_{2})\phi\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+|\zeta_{2}-b_{1}|^{2}\|X_{2}\phi\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\lesssim s^{-2}.

Similar as above, using the choice of final data (
3.9
), we also have

\|V^{in}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}=2\|Q\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+O\left((s^{in})^{-2}\right)=2\|Q\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+O\left(s^{-2}\right).

Combining the above estimates with the conservation law of mass, we complete the proof for the
L^{2}
estimate. The
L^{\infty}
estimate then follows directly from the bootstrap assumption (
3.13
) and the Gagliardo-Nirenberg inequality.
∎

We now deduce the equation of the remainder term
\varepsilon
from (
1.4
) and Proposition
3.1
.

Lemma 3.4

(Equation of
\varepsilon
)
.

It holds

\displaystyle\partial_{s}\varepsilon+\partial_{y}\left(\partial_{y}^{2}\varepsilon-\varepsilon+(V+\varepsilon)^{5}-V^{5}\right)+\Psi(V)

(3.16)

\displaystyle=\frac{\dot{\lambda}}{\lambda}\Lambda\varepsilon+\bigg(\frac{\dot{\lambda}}{\lambda}+b_{1}\bigg)\Lambda V+\left(\frac{\dot{x}_{1}}{\lambda}-1\right)\left(\partial_{y}V+\partial_{y}\varepsilon\right).

Proof.

First, from (
1.4
) and (
3.3
), we find

\displaystyle\partial_{s}(V+\varepsilon)+\partial_{y}\left(\partial_{y}^{2}(V+\varepsilon)-(V+\varepsilon)+(V+\varepsilon)^{5}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\dot{\lambda}}{\lambda}(\Lambda V+\Lambda\varepsilon)+\left(\frac{\dot{x}_{1}}{\lambda}-1\right)(\partial_{y}V+\partial_{y}\varepsilon),

which directly implies

\displaystyle\partial_{s}\varepsilon+\partial_{y}\left(\partial_{y}^{2}\varepsilon-\varepsilon+(V+\varepsilon)^{5}-V^{5}\right)+\Psi(V)

\displaystyle+\partial_{s}V+\partial_{y}\left(\partial_{y}^{2}V-V+V^{5}\right)+b_{1}\Lambda V-\Psi(V)

\displaystyle=\frac{\dot{\lambda}}{\lambda}\Lambda\varepsilon+\bigg(\frac{\dot{\lambda}}{\lambda}+b_{1}\bigg)\Lambda V+\left(\frac{\dot{x}_{1}}{\lambda}-1\right)\left(\partial_{y}V+\partial_{y}\varepsilon\right).

Combining the above identity with (
2.28
), we complete the proof for (
3.16
).
∎

Note that, from (
3.13
) and the definition
b_{2}=\zeta_{2}(1+\mu)^{\frac{3}{2}}
, we compute

\dot{\zeta}_{2}=\frac{\dot{b}_{2}}{(1+\mu)^{\frac{3}{2}}}-\frac{3\dot{\mu}b_{1}}{2(1+\mu)^{\frac{5}{2}}}\Longrightarrow\dot{\zeta}_{2}=\dot{b}_{2}+O\left(\frac{|\dot{b}_{2}|}{s}+\frac{|\dot{\mu}|}{s\log s}\right).

(3.17)

The above estimate will be used frequently in the proof of the following Lemma.

Now, we derive the control for
\mathcal{G}(s)
from the orthogonality conditions (
3.4
).

Lemma 3.5

(Control for parameters)
.

The following estimates hold on
[s^{*},s^{in}]
.

(i)

Estimate on
(\lambda,z,\mu,x_{1})
. We have

\displaystyle\Big|\dot{z}-b_{1}z-\mu+z\Big(\frac{\dot{\lambda}}{\lambda}+b_{1}\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{C_{0}}{s^{\frac{3}{2}}\log s},

\displaystyle\bigg|\frac{\dot{\lambda}}{\lambda}+b_{1}\bigg|+\bigg|\frac{\dot{x}_{1}}{\lambda}-1\bigg|+|\dot{\mu}-2\Theta|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{C_{0}}{s^{\frac{3}{2}}\log s}.

(ii)

Estimate on
(b_{1},\zeta_{2})
.
We have

\displaystyle|\dot{b}_{1}+\alpha e^{-z}+2b_{1}^{2}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{C_{0}}{s^{\frac{5}{2}}\log^{2}s}+\frac{C_{0}^{2}}{s^{3}\log^{2}s},

\displaystyle|\dot{\zeta}_{2}+\alpha e^{-z}+2b_{1}^{2}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{C_{0}}{s^{\frac{5}{2}}\log^{2}s}+\frac{C_{0}^{2}}{s^{3}\log^{2}s}.

Proof.

Step 1.
Estimate on
(\lambda,x_{1})
.
From Lemma
3.4
and the orthogonality condition
(\varepsilon(s),\Lambda Q)=0
, we check that

\displaystyle\left(\partial_{y}\left(\partial_{y}^{2}\varepsilon-\varepsilon+(V+\varepsilon)^{5}-V^{5}\right),\Lambda Q\right)+\left(\Psi(V),\Lambda Q\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\dot{\lambda}}{\lambda}\left(\Lambda\varepsilon,\Lambda Q\right)+\bigg(\frac{\dot{\lambda}}{\lambda}+b_{1}\bigg)\left(\Lambda V,\Lambda Q\right)+\left(\frac{\dot{x}_{1}}{\lambda}-1\right)\left(\left(\partial_{y}V+\partial_{y}\varepsilon\right),\Lambda Q\right).

First, from the bootstrap assumption (
3.13
) and Lemma
3.3
, we find

\displaystyle\left|\left(\partial_{y}\left(\partial_{y}^{2}\varepsilon-\varepsilon+(V+\varepsilon)^{5}-V^{5}\right),\Lambda Q\right)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\|V\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{\infty}}^{3}\|\Lambda Q\|_{L^{2}}+\|V\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{\infty}}^{4}\|\Lambda Q\|_{L^{2}}+\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{\infty}}^{5}\|\Lambda Q\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+(1+\|V\|_{L^{\infty}}^{4})\left|\left(\varepsilon,\partial^{\leq 3}_{y}\Lambda Q\right)\right|+\|V\|_{L^{\infty}}^{3}\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\|\Lambda Q\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\frac{C_{0}}{s^{\frac{3}{2}}\log s}.

Then, using
(\Lambda Q,Q^{\prime})=0
the definition of
V
in (
2.28
), we check that

\displaystyle(\partial_{y}V,\Lambda Q)

\displaystyle=O\left(|b_{1}|+|\Theta|+zb_{1}^{2}+z^{5}e^{-z}\right),

\displaystyle(\Lambda V,\Lambda Q)

\displaystyle=\|\Lambda Q\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+O\left(|b_{1}|+|\Theta|+zb_{1}^{2}+z^{5}e^{-z}\right).

On the other hand, from Lemma
2.20
and bootstrap assumption (
3.13
),

\displaystyle\left(\Psi(V),\Lambda Q\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\vec{{\rm{Mod}}}\cdot\vec{M}Q+\sum_{i=1}^{4}\Psi_{i}(V)+O_{\mathcal{S}}(1),\Lambda Q\right)

\displaystyle=O\left(\frac{|\vec{{\rm{Mod}}}|}{s\log s}+|\dot{b}_{1}+\alpha e^{-z}+2b_{1}^{2}|+\frac{1}{s^{3}}\right).

Here, we use the fact that

f\in\mathcal{S}\Longrightarrow\|f\|_{H^{1}}\lesssim\frac{|\vec{{\rm{Mod}}}|}{s\log s}+\frac{1}{s^{3}\log s}.

Next, we rewrite

\displaystyle\frac{\dot{\lambda}}{\lambda}\left(\Lambda\varepsilon,\Lambda Q\right)

\displaystyle=\bigg(\frac{\dot{\lambda}}{\lambda}+b_{1}\bigg)\left(\Lambda\varepsilon,\Lambda Q\right)-b_{1}(\Lambda\varepsilon,\Lambda Q)

\displaystyle=O\bigg(\frac{C_{0}}{s^{\frac{3}{2}}\log s}\bigg|\frac{\dot{\lambda}}{\lambda}+b_{1}\bigg|+\frac{C_{0}}{s^{\frac{5}{2}}\log^{2}s}\bigg).

It follows from the bootstrap assumption (
3.13
) that

\bigg|\frac{\dot{\lambda}}{\lambda}+b_{1}\bigg|\lesssim\frac{|\vec{{\rm{Mod}}}|}{s\log s}+\frac{1}{s\log s}\left|\frac{\dot{x}_{1}}{\lambda}-1\right|+|\dot{b}_{1}+\alpha e^{-z}+2b_{1}^{2}|+\frac{C_{0}}{s^{\frac{3}{2}}\log s}.

Similarly, using the orthogonality condition
(\varepsilon(s),\partial_{y}Q_{1})=0
, we find

\bigg|\frac{\dot{x}_{1}}{\lambda}-1\bigg|\lesssim\frac{|\vec{{\rm{Mod}}}|}{s\log s}+\frac{1}{s\log s}\bigg|\frac{\dot{\lambda}}{\lambda}+b_{1}\bigg|+|\dot{b}_{1}+\alpha e^{-z}+2b_{1}^{2}|+\frac{C_{0}}{s\log^{\frac{3}{2}}s}.

Combining the above estimates, we deduce that

\bigg|\frac{\dot{\lambda}}{\lambda}+b_{1}\bigg|+\left|\frac{\dot{x}_{1}}{\lambda}-1\right|\lesssim\frac{|\vec{{\rm{Mod}}}|}{s\log s}+|\dot{b}_{1}+\alpha e^{-z}+2b_{1}^{2}|+\frac{C_{0}}{s^{\frac{3}{2}}\log s}.

(3.18)

Step 2.
Estimate on
\mu
. From Lemma
3.4
and the orthogonality condition
(\varepsilon(s),\Gamma\Lambda Q)=0
, we check that

\displaystyle\left(\partial_{y}\left(\partial_{y}^{2}\varepsilon-\varepsilon+(V+\varepsilon)^{5}-V^{5}\right),\Gamma\Lambda Q\right)+\left(\Psi(V),\Gamma\Lambda Q\right)

\displaystyle=\bigg(\frac{\dot{\lambda}}{\lambda}+b_{1}\bigg)\left(\Lambda V,\Gamma\Lambda Q\right)+\left(\frac{\dot{x}_{1}}{\lambda}-1\right)\left(\left(\partial_{y}V+\partial_{y}\varepsilon\right),\Gamma\Lambda Q\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{\dot{\lambda}}{\lambda}\left(\Lambda\varepsilon,\Gamma\Lambda Q\right)-\dot{z}\left(\varepsilon,\partial_{y}\Gamma\Lambda Q\right)+\frac{\dot{\mu}}{2(1+\mu)}(\varepsilon,\Gamma(\Lambda\Lambda Q)).

First, from the bootstrap assumption (
3.13
) and Lemma
3.3
, we find

\left|\left(\partial_{y}\left(\partial_{y}^{2}\varepsilon-\varepsilon+(V+\varepsilon)^{5}-V^{5}\right),\Gamma\Lambda Q\right)\right|\lesssim\frac{C_{0}}{s^{\frac{3}{2}}\log s}.

Then, using again
(\Lambda Q,Q^{\prime})=0
and
the definition of
V
in (
2.28
),

\displaystyle(\partial_{y}V,\Gamma\Lambda Q)

\displaystyle=O\left(|b_{1}|+|\Theta|+zb_{1}^{2}+z^{5}e^{-z}\right),

\displaystyle(\Lambda V,\Gamma\Lambda Q)

\displaystyle=-\|\Lambda Q\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+O\left(|b_{1}|+|\Theta|+zb_{1}^{2}+z^{5}e^{-z}\right).

On the other hand, from Lemma
2.20
and bootstrap assumption (
3.13
),

\displaystyle\left(\Psi(V),\Gamma\Lambda Q\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\vec{{\rm{Mod}}}\cdot\vec{M}Q+\sum_{i=1}^{4}\Psi_{i}(V)+O_{\mathcal{S}}(1),\Gamma\Lambda Q\right)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}(\dot{\mu}-2\Theta)\left(\|\Lambda Q\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+O(s^{-1})\right)+O\left(\frac{|\vec{\rm{Mod}}|}{s\log s}+\frac{1}{s^{3}}\right)

\displaystyle+O\left(|\dot{b}_{1}+2\alpha e^{-z}+2b_{1}^{2}|+|\dot{\zeta}_{2}+2\alpha e^{-z}+2b_{1}^{2}|\right).

Next, using again the bootstrap assumption (
3.13
), we find

\displaystyle\bigg|\frac{\dot{\lambda}}{\lambda}\left(\Lambda\varepsilon,\Gamma\Lambda Q\right)\bigg|+\left|\dot{z}\left(\varepsilon,\partial_{y}\Gamma\Lambda Q\right)\right|+\left|\frac{\dot{\mu}}{1+\mu}(\varepsilon,\Gamma(\Lambda\Lambda Q))\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{C_{0}}{s^{\frac{3}{2}}\log s}\bigg|\frac{\dot{\lambda}}{\lambda}+b_{1}\bigg|+\frac{|\vec{\rm{Mod}}|}{s\log s}+\frac{C_{0}}{s^{\frac{5}{2}}\log s}+\frac{C_{0}}{s^{\frac{5}{2}}\log^{2}s}.

Combining the above estimates with (
3.18
), we deduce that

\displaystyle|\dot{\mu}-2\Theta|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{|\vec{{\rm{Mod}}}|}{s\log s}+|\dot{b}_{1}+2\alpha e^{-z}+2b_{1}^{2}|

(3.19)

\displaystyle+\frac{C_{0}}{s^{\frac{3}{2}}\log s}+|\dot{\zeta}_{2}+2\alpha e^{-z}+2b_{1}^{2}|.

Step 3.
Estimate on
z
. From Lemma
3.4
and the orthogonality condition
(\varepsilon(s),\partial_{y}Q_{2})=0
, we check that

\displaystyle\left(\partial_{y}\left(\partial_{y}^{2}\varepsilon-\varepsilon+(V+\varepsilon)^{5}-V^{5}\right),\partial_{y}Q_{2}\right)+\left(\Psi(V),\partial_{y}Q_{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\bigg(\frac{\dot{\lambda}}{\lambda}+b_{1}\bigg)\left(\Lambda V,\partial_{y}Q_{2}\right)+\left(\frac{\dot{x}_{1}}{\lambda}-1\right)\left(\left(\partial_{y}V+\partial_{y}\varepsilon\right),\partial_{y}Q_{2}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{\dot{\lambda}}{\lambda}\left(\Lambda\varepsilon,\partial_{y}Q_{2}\right)-\dot{z}\left(\varepsilon,\partial^{2}_{y}Q_{2}\right)+\frac{\dot{\mu}}{2(1+\mu)}(\varepsilon,\Gamma(\Lambda Q^{\prime})).

First, from the bootstrap assumption (
3.13
) and Lemma
3.3
, we find

\left|\left(\partial_{y}\left(\partial_{y}^{2}\varepsilon-\varepsilon+(V+\varepsilon)^{5}-V^{5}\right),\partial_{y}Q_{2}\right)\right|\lesssim\frac{C_{0}}{s^{\frac{3}{2}}\log s}.

Then, using again
(\Lambda Q,Q^{\prime})=0
and
the definition of
V
in (
2.28
),

\displaystyle(\partial_{y}V,\partial_{y}Q_{2})

\displaystyle=-\|Q^{\prime}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+O\left(|b_{1}|+|\Theta|+|\mu|+zb_{1}^{2}+z^{5}e^{-z}\right),

\displaystyle(\Lambda V,\partial_{y}Q_{2})

\displaystyle=-z\|Q^{\prime}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+O\left(|b_{1}|+|\Theta|+|\mu|z+zb_{1}^{2}+z^{5}e^{-z}\right).

On the other hand, from Lemma
2.20
and bootstrap assumption (
3.13
),

\displaystyle\left(\Psi(V),\partial_{y}Q_{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\vec{{\rm{Mod}}}\cdot\vec{M}Q+\sum_{i=1}^{4}\Psi_{i}(V)+O_{\mathcal{S}}(1),\partial_{y}Q_{2}\right)

\displaystyle=(\dot{z}-b_{1}z-\mu)\|Q^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+O\left(\frac{|\vec{\rm{Mod}}|}{s\log s}+\frac{1}{s^{3}}\right)

\displaystyle+O\left(|\dot{b}_{1}+2\alpha e^{-z}+2b_{1}^{2}|+|\dot{\zeta}_{2}+2\alpha e^{-z}+2b_{1}^{2}|\right).

Next, using again the bootstrap assumption (
3.13
), we find

\displaystyle\bigg|\frac{\dot{\lambda}}{\lambda}\left(\Lambda\varepsilon,\partial_{y}Q_{2}\right)\bigg|+\left|\dot{z}\left(\varepsilon,\partial^{2}_{y}Q_{2}\right)\right|+\left|\frac{\dot{\mu}}{1+\mu}(\varepsilon,\Gamma(\Lambda Q^{\prime}))\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{C_{0}}{s^{\frac{3}{2}}\log s}\bigg|\frac{\dot{\lambda}}{\lambda}+b_{1}\bigg|+\frac{|\vec{\rm{Mod}}|}{s\log s}+\frac{C_{0}}{s^{\frac{5}{2}}\log s}+\frac{C_{0}}{s^{\frac{5}{2}}\log^{2}s}.

Combining the above estimates with (
3.18
), we deduce that

\displaystyle\bigg|\dot{z}-b_{1}z-\mu+z\bigg(\frac{\dot{\lambda}}{\lambda}+b_{1}\bigg)\bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{|\vec{{\rm{Mod}}}|}{s\log s}+|\dot{b}_{1}+2\alpha e^{-z}+2b_{1}^{2}|

(3.20)

\displaystyle+\frac{C_{0}}{s^{\frac{3}{2}}\log s}+|\dot{\zeta}_{2}+2\alpha e^{-z}+2b_{1}^{2}|.

Step 4.
Estimates on
(b_{1},\zeta_{2})
. From Lemma
3.4
and the orthogonality condition
(\varepsilon(s),Q)=0
, we check that

\displaystyle\left(\partial_{y}\left(\partial_{y}^{2}\varepsilon-\varepsilon+(V+\varepsilon)^{5}-V^{5}\right),Q\right)+\left(\Psi(V),Q\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\dot{\lambda}}{\lambda}\left(\Lambda\varepsilon,Q\right)+\bigg(\frac{\dot{\lambda}}{\lambda}+b_{1}\bigg)\left(\Lambda V,Q\right)+\left(\frac{\dot{x}_{1}}{\lambda}-1\right)\left(\left(\partial_{y}V+\partial_{y}\varepsilon\right),Q\right).

First, we rewrite

\displaystyle\partial_{y}^{2}\varepsilon-\varepsilon+(V+\varepsilon)^{5}-V^{5}

\displaystyle=-\mathcal{L}\varepsilon+5(V^{4}-Q^{4})\varepsilon

\displaystyle+\left((V+\varepsilon)^{5}-V^{5}-5V^{4}\varepsilon\right).

It follows from (
3.13
), Lemma
3.3
and
{\rm{Ker}}\mathcal{L}={\rm{Span}}\{Q^{\prime}\}
that

\displaystyle\left|\left(\partial_{y}\left(\partial_{y}^{2}\varepsilon-\varepsilon+(V+\varepsilon)^{5}-V^{5}\right),Q\right)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\|V\|_{L^{\infty}}^{3}+\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\right)|(\varepsilon^{2},Q^{\prime})|+|((S^{4}-Q^{4})\varepsilon,Q^{\prime})|

\displaystyle+\left(\|R\phi\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|U\|_{L^{\infty}}\right)|\|\varepsilon Q^{\prime}\|_{L^{1}}+\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{\infty}}^{5}\lesssim\frac{C_{0}}{s^{\frac{5}{2}}\log^{2}s}+\frac{C_{0}^{2}}{s^{3}\log^{2}s}.

Here, we use the fact that

\|\varepsilon Q^{\prime}\|_{L^{1}}+\|\varepsilon Q_{2}\|_{L^{1}}+|(\varepsilon^{2},Q^{\prime})|^{\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim\mathcal{N}_{B}(\varepsilon).

Then, using again
(Q^{\prime},Q)=(\Lambda Q,Q)=0
and
the definition of
V
in (
2.28
),

\displaystyle(\partial_{y}V,Q)

\displaystyle=O\left(|b_{1}|+|\Theta|+zb_{1}^{2}+z^{5}e^{-z}\right),

\displaystyle(\Lambda V,Q)

\displaystyle=O\left(|b_{1}|+|\Theta|+zb_{1}^{2}+z^{5}e^{-z}\right).

On the other hand, from Lemma
2.20
and bootstrap assumption (
3.13
),

\displaystyle\left(\Psi(V),Q\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\vec{{\rm{Mod}}}\cdot\vec{M}Q+\sum_{i=1}^{4}\Psi_{i}(V)+O_{\mathcal{S}}(1),Q\right)

\displaystyle=\left(-m_{0}^{2}+O(s^{-1})\right)\big(\dot{b}_{1}+\alpha e^{-z}+2b_{1}^{2}\big)+\frac{1}{s^{3}}

\displaystyle+O\left(\frac{1}{s}|\dot{\zeta}_{2}+\alpha e^{-z}+2b_{1}^{2}|\right)+\frac{|\vec{{\rm{Mod}}}|}{s^{\frac{3}{2}}\log s}.

Next, using integration by parts,

(\varepsilon,\Lambda Q)=(\varepsilon,Q^{\prime})=0\Longrightarrow\left(\Lambda\varepsilon,Q\right)=(\partial_{y}\varepsilon,Q)=0.

Combining the above estimates with (
3.18
), we have

\displaystyle|\dot{b}_{1}+\alpha e^{-z}+2b_{1}^{2}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{|\vec{{\rm{Mod}}}|}{s^{\frac{3}{2}}\log s}+\frac{1}{s^{3}}+\frac{C_{0}^{2}}{s^{3}\log^{2}s}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{s}|\dot{\zeta}_{2}+\alpha e^{-z}+2b_{1}^{2}|+\frac{C_{0}}{s^{\frac{5}{2}}\log^{2}s}.

Based on a similar argument as above, we also deduce that

\displaystyle|\dot{\zeta}_{2}+\alpha e^{-z}+2b_{1}^{2}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{|\vec{{\rm{Mod}}}|}{s^{\frac{3}{2}}\log s}+\frac{1}{s^{3}}+\frac{C_{0}^{2}}{s^{3}\log^{2}s}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{s}|\dot{b}_{1}+\alpha e^{-z}+2b_{1}^{2}|+\frac{C_{0}}{s^{\frac{5}{2}}\log^{2}s}.

Combining the above two estimates, we deduce that

\displaystyle|\dot{b}_{1}+\alpha e^{-z}+2b_{1}^{2}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{|\vec{{\rm{Mod}}}|}{s^{\frac{3}{2}}\log s}+\frac{1}{s^{3}}+\frac{C_{0}}{s^{\frac{5}{2}}\log^{2}s}+\frac{C_{0}^{2}}{s^{3}\log^{2}s},

(3.21)

\displaystyle|\dot{\zeta}_{2}+\alpha e^{-z}+2b_{1}^{2}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{|\vec{{\rm{Mod}}}|}{s^{\frac{3}{2}}\log s}+\frac{1}{s^{3}}+\frac{C_{0}}{s^{\frac{5}{2}}\log^{2}s}+\frac{C_{0}^{2}}{s^{3}\log^{2}s}.

Step 5.
Conclusion. First, from (
3.19
)–(
3.21
), we have

\displaystyle\bigg|\dot{z}-b_{1}z-\mu+z\bigg(\frac{\dot{\lambda}}{\lambda}+b_{1}\bigg)\bigg|+|\dot{\mu}-2\Theta|

(3.22)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{|\vec{{\rm{Mod}}}|}{s\log s}+\frac{C_{0}}{s^{\frac{3}{2}}\log s}+\frac{1}{s^{3}}+\frac{C_{0}^{2}}{s^{3}\log^{2}s},

which directly implies that

\displaystyle\bigg|\dot{z}-b_{1}z-\mu\bigg|+|\dot{\mu}-2\Theta|\lesssim\frac{|\vec{{\rm{Mod}}}|}{s}+\frac{C_{0}}{s^{\frac{3}{2}}}+\frac{\log s}{s^{3}}+\frac{C_{0}^{2}}{s^{3}\log s}.

(3.23)

Combining the above estimate with (
3.21
), we deduce that

|\vec{{\rm{Mod}}}|\lesssim\frac{C_{0}}{s^{\frac{3}{2}}}+\frac{\log s}{s^{3}}+\frac{C_{0}^{2}}{s^{3}\log s}\lesssim\frac{C_{0}}{s^{\frac{3}{2}}}.

(3.24)

We see that (i) and (ii) follows directly from (
3.21
)–(
3.24
) and
s_{0}
large enough.
∎

Based on Lemma
2.19
and Lemma
3.5
, we directly obtain the following estimates.

Lemma 3.6
.

The following estimate hold.

(i)

Estimate for
\Psi_{1}(V)
.
We have

\Psi_{1}(V)=\left(\dot{z}-b_{1}z-\mu\right)(\zeta_{2}\partial_{y}X_{2}+10m_{0}b_{1}\partial_{y}Y_{2})+O_{H^{1}}\left(\frac{1}{s^{\frac{5}{2}}\log s}\right).

(ii)

Standard estimate for
\Psi_{2}(V)-\Psi_{4}(V)
.
We have

\|\Psi_{2}(V)\|_{\dot{H}^{1}}+\|\Psi_{3}(V)\|_{H^{1}}+\|\Psi_{4}(V)\|_{\dot{H}^{1}}\lesssim\frac{1}{s^{\frac{5}{2}}\log s}.

(iii)

Weighted
L^{2}
estimate for
\Psi_{2}(V)
and
\Psi_{4}(V)
.
We have

\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}|\Psi_{2}(V)|^{2}\phi_{1}{\rm d}y\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}|\Psi_{4}(V)|^{2}\phi_{1}{\rm d}y\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim\frac{1}{s^{\frac{5}{2}}\log s}.

Proof.

Proof of (i) and (ii). These estimates follow directly from Lemma
2.19
, Lemma
3.5
, and the expansion of
\Psi_{1}(V)
in Proposition
2.18
.

Proof of (iii). First, from Lemma
2.19
and Lemma
3.5
, we have

\Psi_{2}(V)=-\dot{z}e^{-z}\left(A_{1}+B_{2}\right)\phi+\frac{\dot{\mu}}{2}b_{1}^{2}\phi\Gamma(\Lambda F)+O_{L^{2}}\left(\frac{1}{s^{\frac{5}{2}}\log s}\right).

Then, we rewrite again

\displaystyle A_{1}+B_{2}

\displaystyle=(A_{1}-2\alpha m_{0}y)+2\alpha m_{0}y\big(1-(1+\mu)^{\frac{3}{4}}\big)

\displaystyle+(B_{2}+2\alpha m_{0}(1+\mu)^{\frac{3}{4}}(y-z))+2\alpha m_{0}(1+\mu)^{\frac{3}{4}}z,

which implies that

|(A_{1}+B_{2})\phi|\lesssim z\textbf{1}_{\left(-\infty,\frac{5}{2}s\right]}(y)\lesssim(\log s)\textbf{1}_{\left(-\infty,\frac{5}{2}s\right]}(y).

Combining the above estimate with (
3.12
) and Lemma
3.5
, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}|\dot{z}e^{-z}\left(A_{1}+B_{2}\right)\phi|^{2}\phi_{1}{\rm d}y

\displaystyle\lesssim s^{-6}\int_{-\infty}^{B(s^{\frac{1}{2}}+2)}|A_{1}+B_{2}|\phi{\rm d}y

\displaystyle+s^{-4}\int_{B(s^{\frac{1}{2}}+2)}^{\frac{52}{s}}\phi_{1}{\rm d}y\lesssim s^{-\frac{11}{2}}.

Similar as above, we also obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}|\dot{\mu}b_{1}^{2}\phi\Gamma(\Lambda F)|^{2}\phi_{1}{\rm d}y

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{C_{0}^{2}}{s^{7}\log^{6}s}\int_{\mathbb{R}}|\phi\Gamma(\Lambda F)|^{2}\phi_{1}{\rm d}y

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{C_{0}^{2}}{s^{7}\log^{6}s}\int^{\frac{5s}{2}}_{B(s^{\frac{1}{2}}+2)}\left(\left|\frac{y}{B}-s^{\frac{1}{2}}\right|^{2}+s\right)\phi_{1}{\rm d}y

\displaystyle+\frac{C_{0}^{2}}{s^{7}\log^{6}s}\int_{-\infty}^{B(s^{\frac{1}{2}}+2)}|\Gamma(\Lambda F)|^{2}{\rm d}y\lesssim\frac{C_{0}^{2}}{s^{\frac{11}{2}}\log^{6}s}.

In addition, from (iv) of Lemma
2.19
, we obtain

\int_{\mathbb{R}}|\Psi_{4}(V)|^{2}\phi_{1}{\rm d}y\lesssim s^{-6}\int_{\frac{s}{2}}^{\frac{5s}{2}}\phi_{1}{\rm d}y\lesssim s^{-6}.

We see that (ii) follows directly from the above estimates.
∎

Last, we derive the refined control for the evolution of parameters
(\mu,\Theta)
, which is inspired by the previous work of
[
33
, (iv) of Lemma 2.7]
on the study of near soliton dynamics of the mass-critical gKdV equation. We denote

\mathcal{J}_{1}(s)=\frac{1}{m_{0}^{2}}\left(\varepsilon(s),\rho_{1}(s)\right)\ \ \mbox{with}\ \ \rho_{1}(s,y)=\int_{y}^{\infty}\left(\Lambda Q(\sigma)-(\Lambda Q)(\sigma-z)\right){\rm d}\sigma.

(3.25)

Thanks to the exponential decay of
\rho
on both sides of real axis
\mathbb{R}
, the quantity
\mathcal{J}(s)
is well-defined for any
\varepsilon(s)\in H^{1}
over the time interval
[s^{*},s^{in}]
.
More precisely, from the exponential decay of
Q
, for
y>z
, we have

|\rho_{1}(s,y)|\lesssim\int_{y}^{\infty}|\Lambda Q(\sigma)|{\rm d}\sigma+\int_{y-z}^{\infty}|\Lambda Q(\sigma)|{\rm d}\sigma\lesssim e^{-\frac{1}{2}|y-z|}.

(3.26)

On the other hand, using again the exponential decay of
Q
, for
y<0
, we have

|\rho_{1}(s,y)|\lesssim\int_{-\infty}^{y}|\Lambda Q(\sigma)|{\rm d}\sigma+\int_{-\infty}^{y-z}|\Lambda Q(\sigma)|{\rm d}\sigma\lesssim e^{-\frac{1}{2}|y|}.

(3.27)

It follows directly that

|\rho_{1}(s,y)|\lesssim e^{-\frac{1}{2}|y|}\textbf{1}_{(-\infty,0)}+\textbf{1}_{[0,z]}+e^{-\frac{1}{2}|y-z|}\textbf{1}_{(z,\infty)}\lesssim\phi_{1}(s,y)\quad\mbox{on}\ \mathbb{R}.

(3.28)

Lemma 3.7

(Refined control of
\mu
)
.

On
[s^{*},s^{in}]
, we have

\big|\dot{\mu}-2\Theta+\dot{\mathcal{J}}_{1}\big|\lesssim\frac{1}{s^{\frac{5}{2}}\log^{\frac{1}{2}}s}+\frac{C_{0}}{s^{\frac{5}{2}}\log s}++\frac{C_{0}^{2}}{s^{3}\log s}.

To complete the proof of above Lemma, we need the following technical estimates.

Lemma 3.8
.

The following estimates hold on
[s^{*},s^{in}]
.

(i)

Estimates related to
\varepsilon
.
We have

\left|(\partial_{y}\varepsilon,\rho_{1})\right|+\left|(\Lambda\varepsilon,\rho_{1})\right|\lesssim\frac{C_{0}}{s^{\frac{3}{2}}}.

(ii)

Estimates related to
Q
.
We have

\left(\Lambda Q,\rho_{1}\right)=(\Gamma(\Lambda Q),\rho_{1})=-2m_{0}^{2}+O\left(s^{-1}\right).

(iii)

First estimate related to
V
.

We have

\left|(\partial_{y}V,\rho_{1})\right|\lesssim s^{-1}.

(iv)

Second estimate related to
V
.

We have

(\Lambda V,\rho_{1})=z(Q_{2},\partial_{y}\rho_{1})+O\left(s^{-1}\right).

(v)

Estimates related to
\Psi(V)
.
We have

\displaystyle\left(\Psi(V),\rho_{1}\right)

\displaystyle=m_{0}^{2}(\dot{\mu}-\Theta)-(\dot{z}-b_{1}z-\mu)\left(Q_{2},\partial_{y}\rho_{1}\right)

\displaystyle+O\left(\frac{1}{s^{\frac{5}{2}}\log^{\frac{1}{2}}s}+\frac{C_{0}}{s^{\frac{5}{2}}\log s}+\frac{C_{0}^{2}}{s^{3}\log s}\right).

Proof.

Proof of (i). From integration by parts and the bootstrap assumption (
3.13
),

\displaystyle(\partial_{y}\varepsilon,\rho_{1})

\displaystyle=(\varepsilon,\Lambda Q-(\Lambda Q)(\cdot-z))=O\left(\frac{C_{0}}{s^{\frac{3}{2}}}\right),

\displaystyle(\Lambda\varepsilon,\rho_{1})

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}(\varepsilon,\rho_{1})+(\varepsilon,y\partial_{y}\rho_{1})=-\frac{1}{2}(\varepsilon,\rho_{1})+O\left(\frac{C_{0}}{s^{\frac{3}{2}}}\right).

Here, we use the fact that

|(1+|y|)\Lambda Q|+|(1+|y-z|)\Gamma\Lambda Q|\lesssim\phi_{1},\quad\mbox{on}\ \mathbb{R}.

Then, from (
3.13
) and (
3.26
)–(
3.28
), we check that

\displaystyle\left|\left(\varepsilon,\rho_{1}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\varepsilon^{2}\rho_{1}{\rm d}y\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\rho_{1}{\rm d}y\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim\frac{C_{0}}{s^{\frac{3}{2}}\log^{\frac{1}{2}}s}.

Combining the above estimates, we complete the proof for (i).

Proof of (ii). First, from the Fubini’s Theorem and
\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Lambda Q(y){\rm d}y=-2m_{0}
,

\displaystyle\Big(\Lambda Q,\int_{y}^{\infty}\Lambda Q(\sigma){\rm d}\sigma\Big)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\Lambda Q(y)\Lambda Q(\sigma)\textbf{1}_{\{y\leq\sigma\}}{\rm d}y{\rm d}\sigma

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Lambda Q(y){\rm d}y\right)^{2}=2m_{0}^{2}.

Second, from the exponential decay of
Q
, we check that

\displaystyle\int_{y}^{\infty}(\Lambda Q)(\sigma-z){\rm d}\sigma

\displaystyle=O\left(\langle y-z\rangle e^{-|y-z|}\right),\qquad\qquad\ \ \ \mbox{for}\ \ y>z,

\displaystyle\int_{y}^{\infty}(\Lambda Q)(\sigma-z){\rm d}\sigma

\displaystyle=-2m_{0}+O\left(\langle y-z\rangle e^{-|y-z|}\right),\quad\mbox{for}\ \ y<z.

Based on the above estimate and the bootstrap assumption (
3.14
), we find

\displaystyle\int_{z}^{\infty}\int_{y}^{\infty}\left(\Lambda Q(y)\right)\left((\Lambda Q)(\sigma-z)\right){\rm d}\sigma{\rm d}y

\displaystyle=O\Big(s^{-\frac{3}{2}}\Big),

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{z}\int_{y}^{\infty}\left(\Lambda Q(y)\right)\left((\Lambda Q)(\sigma-z)\right){\rm d}\sigma{\rm d}y

\displaystyle=4m_{0}^{2}+O\Big(s^{-\frac{3}{2}}\Big),

which directly implies

\Big(\Lambda Q,\int_{y}^{\infty}\Lambda Q(\sigma-z){\rm d}\sigma\Big)=4m_{0}^{2}+O\Big(s^{-\frac{3}{2}}\Big).

Here, we use the fact that

\displaystyle\int_{z}^{\infty}\langle y\rangle\langle y-z\rangle e^{-|y|-|y-z|}{\rm d}y\lesssim e^{-\frac{3}{4}z}

\displaystyle\lesssim s^{-\frac{3}{2}},

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{z}\langle y\rangle\langle y-z\rangle e^{-|y|-|y-z|}{\rm d}y\lesssim e^{-\frac{3}{4}z}

\displaystyle\lesssim s^{-\frac{3}{2}}.

Combining the above estimates, we obtain

\displaystyle(\Lambda Q,\rho_{1})

\displaystyle=\Big(\Lambda Q,\int_{y}^{\infty}\Lambda Q(\sigma){\rm d}\sigma\Big)

\displaystyle-\Big(\Lambda Q,\int_{y}^{\infty}\Lambda Q(\sigma-z){\rm d}\sigma\Big)=-2m_{0}^{2}+O\Big(s^{-\frac{3}{2}}\Big).

Similar as above, we also obtain

\displaystyle(\Gamma(\Lambda Q),\rho_{1})

\displaystyle=\Big(\Gamma(\Lambda Q),\int_{y}^{\infty}\Lambda Q(\sigma){\rm d}\sigma\Big)

\displaystyle-\Big(\Gamma\left(\Lambda Q\right),\int_{y}^{\infty}\Lambda Q(\sigma-z){\rm d}\sigma\Big)=-2m_{0}^{2}+O\Big(s^{-1}\Big),

which directly completes the proof for (ii).

Proof of (iii). Using integration by parts, we compute

(\partial_{y}V,\rho)=(V,\Lambda Q)-(V,(\Lambda Q)(\cdot-z)).

Based on the above identity,
(\Lambda Q,Q)=0
and Lemma
2.9
, we obtain

\left|(\partial_{y}V,\rho)\right|\lesssim|\mu|+|b_{1}|+z^{5}e^{-z}\lesssim s^{-1},

which directly completes the proof for (iii).

Proof of (iv). First, we rewrite

\Lambda V=\Lambda Q-\Gamma(\Lambda Q)-z\partial_{y}Q_{2}+\Lambda(R\phi)+\Lambda U.

On the one hand, from (ii) of Lemma
3.8
, we check that

\left(\Lambda Q-\Gamma(\Lambda Q)-z\partial_{y}Q_{2},\rho_{1}\right)=z(Q_{2},\partial_{y}\rho_{1}).

On the other hand, from the bootstrap assumption (
3.13
),

\Lambda(R\phi)+\Lambda U=z\zeta_{2}\partial_{y}X_{2}+10m_{0}b_{1}\partial_{y}Y_{2}+O_{L^{\infty}}\left(\frac{1}{s\log s}\right).

Based on the above estimate and (
3.26
)–(
3.27
), we obtain

\displaystyle\left|\left(\Lambda(R\phi)+\Lambda U,\rho_{1}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim s^{-1}\|\partial_{y}X_{2}\|_{L^{1}}+s^{-1}\|\partial_{y}Y_{2}\|_{L^{1}}+\frac{\|\rho_{1}\|_{L^{1}}}{s\log s}\lesssim s^{-1}.

Combining the above estimates, we complete the proof for (iv).

Proof of (iv). Note that, from Proposition
2.18
and Lemma
3.6
, we find

\displaystyle\Psi(V)

\displaystyle=\left(\dot{z}-b_{1}z-\mu\right)(\zeta_{2}\partial_{y}X_{2}+10m_{0}b_{1}\partial_{y}Y_{2})

\displaystyle+\vec{{\rm{Mod}}}\cdot\vec{M}Q+O_{L^{2}_{\phi_{1}}}\left(\frac{1}{s^{\frac{5}{2}}\log s}\right).

Here, we denote

f\in O_{L^{2}_{\phi_{1}}}\left(\frac{1}{s^{\frac{5}{2}}\log s}\right)\Longleftrightarrow\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}|f(y)|^{2}\phi_{1}{\rm d}y\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim\frac{1}{s^{\frac{5}{2}}\log s}.

It follows from (
3.26
)–(
3.28
) that

\displaystyle\left(\Psi(V),\rho_{1}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\dot{z}-b_{1}z-\mu\right)\left((\zeta_{2}\partial_{y}X_{2}+10m_{0}b_{1}\partial_{y}Y_{2}),\rho_{1}\right)

\displaystyle+\left(\vec{{\rm{Mod}}}\cdot\vec{M}Q,\rho_{1}\right)+O\left(\frac{1}{s^{\frac{5}{2}}\log^{\frac{1}{2}}s}\right).

In addition, using a similar argument as in the proof of (iv), we obtain

\displaystyle\left(\Psi(V),\rho_{1}\right)=\left(\vec{{\rm{Mod}}}\cdot\vec{M}Q,\rho_{1}\right)+O\left(\frac{1}{s^{\frac{5}{2}}\log^{\frac{1}{2}}s}+\frac{C_{0}}{s^{\frac{5}{2}}\log s}\right).

(3.29)

Using integration by parts and the definition of
\vec{M}Q
in (
2.32
),

(\dot{z}-b_{1}z-\mu)\left(M_{1Q},\rho_{1}\right)=-(\dot{z}-b_{1}z-\mu)(Q_{2},\partial_{y}\rho_{1}).

Then, using again the bootstrap assumption (
3.13
) and
(
3.26
)–(
3.27
), we have

\left|(M_{2Q},\rho_{1})\right|+\left|(M_{3Q},\rho_{1})\right|\lesssim 1+z\lesssim\log s,

which implies that

\displaystyle\left|(\dot{b}_{1}+\alpha e^{-z}+2b_{1}^{2})(M_{3Q},\rho_{1})\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{C_{0}}{s^{\frac{5}{2}}\log s}+\frac{C_{0}^{2}}{s^{3}\log s},

\displaystyle\left|(\dot{\zeta}_{2}+\alpha(1+\mu)e^{-z}+2b_{1}^{2})(M_{2Q},\rho_{1})\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{C_{0}}{s^{\frac{5}{2}}\log s}+\frac{C_{0}^{2}}{s^{3}\log s}.

Last, from (ii) of Lemma
3.8
and (
3.13
), we find

(M_{4Q},\rho_{1})=-\frac{1}{2}\left(\Gamma(\Lambda Q),\rho_{1}\right)+O(s^{-1})=m_{0}^{2}+O(s^{-1}),

which implies that

(\dot{\mu}-\Theta)\left(M_{4Q},\rho_{1}\right)=m_{0}^{2}(\dot{\mu}-\Theta)+\left(\frac{C_{0}}{s^{\frac{5}{2}}\log s}\right).

Next, from integration by parts, we check that

\left((\zeta\phi\partial_{y}X_{2}+10m_{0}b\partial_{y}Y_{2}),\rho\right)=-\left((\zeta\phi X_{2}+10m_{0}bY_{2}),\partial_{y}\rho\right).

It follows directly from (
3.13
) and Lemma
3.5
that

\left|\left(\dot{z}-bz-\mu\right)\left((\zeta\phi\partial_{y}X_{2}+10m_{0}b\partial_{y}Y_{2}),\rho\right)\right|\lesssim s^{-2}\log^{-1}s.

Combining the above estimates with (
3.29
), we complete the proof for (v).
∎

We are in a position to complete the proof of Lemma
3.7
.

Proof of Lemma
3.7
.

From the definition of
\mathcal{J}_{1}
in (
3.25
), we compute

\dot{\mathcal{J}_{1}}=\frac{1}{m_{0}^{2}}(\partial_{s}\varepsilon,\rho_{1})-\frac{\dot{z}}{m_{0}^{2}}(\varepsilon,(\Lambda Q)(\cdot-z)).

Using Lemma
3.4
, we decompose

\displaystyle\left(\partial_{s}\varepsilon,\rho_{1}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\left(\partial_{y}^{2}\varepsilon-\varepsilon+(V+\varepsilon)^{5}-V^{5}\right),\partial_{y}\rho_{1}\right)+\frac{\dot{\lambda}}{\lambda}\left(\Lambda\varepsilon,\rho_{1}\right)

\displaystyle-\left(\Psi(V),\rho_{1}\right)+\bigg(\frac{\dot{\lambda}}{\lambda}+b_{1}\bigg)\left(\Lambda V,\rho_{1}\right)+\left(\frac{\dot{x}_{1}}{\lambda}-1\right)\left(\left(\partial_{y}V+\partial_{y}\varepsilon\right),\rho_{1}\right).

First, we compute

\displaystyle\partial_{y}^{2}\varepsilon-\varepsilon+(V+\varepsilon)^{5}-V^{5}

\displaystyle=\partial_{y}^{2}\varepsilon-\varepsilon+5(Q_{1}^{4}+Q_{2}^{4})\varepsilon

\displaystyle+5(V^{4}-Q_{1}^{4}-Q_{2}^{4})\varepsilon+10V^{3}\varepsilon^{2}

\displaystyle+10V^{2}\varepsilon^{3}+5V\varepsilon^{4}+\varepsilon^{5}.

Based on (
3.13
),
\mathcal{L}\Lambda Q=-2Q
and
(\varepsilon,Q_{1})=(\varepsilon,Q_{2})=0
, we compute

\displaystyle\left|\left(\partial_{y}^{2}\varepsilon-\varepsilon+5(Q_{1}^{4}+Q_{2}^{4})\varepsilon,\partial_{y}\rho_{1}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim|\mu|\mathcal{N}_{B}(\varepsilon)+ze^{-z}\mathcal{N}_{B}(\varepsilon)\lesssim\frac{C_{0}}{s^{\frac{5}{2}}\log s}.

In addition, using again the bootstrap assumption (
3.13
) and Lemma
3.3
,

\displaystyle\left|\left((V^{4}-Q_{1}^{4}-Q_{2}^{4})\varepsilon,\partial_{y}\rho_{1}\right)\right|+\left|\left(V^{3}\varepsilon^{2},\partial_{y}\rho_{1}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{C_{0}}{s^{\frac{5}{2}}\log^{2}s},

\displaystyle\left(V^{2}\varepsilon^{3},\partial_{y}\rho_{1}\right)+\left|\left(V\varepsilon^{4},\partial_{y}\rho_{1}\right)\right|+\left|\left(\varepsilon^{5},\partial_{y}\rho_{1}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{C_{0}}{s^{\frac{5}{2}}\log^{2}s}.

Then, from (iv)–(v) of Lemma
3.8
and Lemma
3.5

\displaystyle-\left(\Psi(V),\rho_{1}\right)+\bigg(\frac{\dot{\lambda}}{\lambda}+b_{1}\bigg)\left(\Lambda V,\rho_{1}\right)

\displaystyle=-m_{0}^{2}(\dot{\mu}-\Theta)+O\left(\frac{1}{s^{\frac{5}{2}}\log^{\frac{1}{2}}s}+\frac{C_{0}}{s^{\frac{5}{2}}\log s}++\frac{C_{0}^{2}}{s^{3}\log s}\right).

Here, we use the fact that

(Q_{2},\partial_{y}\rho_{1})=(Q_{2},(\Lambda Q)(\cdot-z))+(Q_{2},\Lambda Q)=O\left(s^{-1}\right).

Last, from Lemma
3.5
and the bootstrap assumption (
3.13
),

\displaystyle\bigg|\left(\frac{\dot{x}_{1}}{\lambda}-1\right)\left(\left(\partial_{y}V+\partial_{y}\varepsilon\right),\rho_{1}\right)\bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{C_{0}}{s^{\frac{5}{2}}\log s},

\displaystyle\ \bigg|\frac{\dot{\lambda}}{\lambda}\left(\Lambda\varepsilon,\rho_{1}\right)\bigg|+\bigg|\dot{z}(\varepsilon,(\Lambda Q)(\cdot-z))\bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{C_{0}}{s^{\frac{5}{2}}\log s}.

Combining the above estimates, we complete the proof of Lemma
3.7
.
∎

We denote

\mathcal{J}_{2}(s)=\frac{1}{m_{0}^{2}}(\varepsilon(s),\rho_{2}(s))\quad\mbox{with}\ \ \rho_{2}(s,y)=X-\bar{P}_{2}+2m_{0}.

(3.32)

It follows directly that

|\rho_{2}(s,y)|\lesssim\left(e^{-\frac{1}{2}|y|}+e^{-\frac{1}{2}|y-z|}\right)\left(\textbf{1}_{(-\infty,0)}+\textbf{1}_{(z,\infty)}\right)+\textbf{1}_{[0,z]}\lesssim\phi_{1}(s,y).

(3.33)
