3.4.
Energy functional

Let
\chi_{2}:\mathbb{R}\to[0,1]
be
C^{\infty}
function such that

\chi_{2}\equiv 1\ \ \mbox{on}\ \left[\frac{1}{2},\frac{3}{2}\right]\quad\mbox{and}\quad\chi_{2}\equiv 0\ \ \mbox{on}\ \left(-\infty,\frac{1}{4}\right]\cup\left[\frac{7}{4},\infty\right).

Denote

\phi_{2}(y)=\chi_{2}\left(\frac{y}{z(s)}\right)\Longrightarrow\phi_{2}\equiv 1\ \ \mbox{on}\ \left[\frac{z}{2},\frac{3z}{2}\right].

(3.39)

We consider the nonlinear energy functional for
\varepsilon
:

\mathcal{F}(s,\varepsilon)=\mathcal{F}_{1}(s,\varepsilon)+\mathcal{F}_{2}(s,\varepsilon),

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{1}(s,\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left((\partial_{y}\varepsilon)^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\phi_{1}-\frac{1}{3}\left((V+\varepsilon)^{6}-V^{6}-6V^{5}\varepsilon\right)\right){\rm d}y,

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{2}(s,\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=\mu\int_{\mathbb{R}}\varepsilon^{2}\phi_{2}{\rm d}y-b_{1}\left(\mathcal{J}_{2}+\left(c_{0}+\frac{5}{4}\right)\mathcal{J}_{1}\right)(\varepsilon\phi_{1},X_{1}\phi).

We mention here that, the function
\mathcal{F}
is coercive in
\varepsilon
at the main order and enjoys a monotonicity property adapted to this problem.

Proposition 3.10
.

The following estimates hold on
[s^{*},s^{in}]
.

(i)

Coercivity of
\mathcal{F}
.

We have

\mathcal{N}_{B}^{2}(\varepsilon)\lesssim\mathcal{F}+s^{-\frac{13}{4}}.

(3.40)

(ii)

Time variation of
\mathcal{F}
.
We have

-\frac{{\rm d}\mathcal{F}}{{\rm d}s}\lesssim\frac{C_{0}^{2}}{s^{4}\log^{\frac{5}{2}}s}+\frac{C_{0}}{s^{4}\log^{2}s}.

(3.41)

Proof.

Proof of (i). The proof of the coercivity for
\mathcal{F}
is a standard consequence of the coercivity property in Proposition
2.1
around single soliton with the orthogonality conditions (
3.4
) and an elementary localization argument. Below, we provide
a sketch of the proof for the sake of completeness and for the readers? convenience.

For some
0<\delta\ll 1
, we decompose

\mathcal{F}_{1}=\mathcal{F}_{1,1}+\mathcal{F}_{1,2}+\mathcal{F}_{1,3}+\mathcal{F}_{1,4}+\mathcal{F}_{1,5},

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{1,1}

\displaystyle=5\int_{\mathbb{R}}(S^{4}-V^{4})\varepsilon^{2}{\rm d}y-5\int_{\mathbb{R}}S^{4}\varepsilon^{2}(1-\phi_{1}){\rm d}y,

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{1,2}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{3}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\varepsilon^{3}\left(20V^{3}+15V^{2}\varepsilon+6V\varepsilon^{2}+\varepsilon^{3}\right){\rm d}y,

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{1,3}

\displaystyle=(1-\delta)\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left((\partial_{y}(\varepsilon\sqrt{\phi_{1}}))^{2}+(\varepsilon\sqrt{\phi_{1}})^{2}-5S^{4}(\varepsilon\sqrt{\phi_{1}})^{2}\right){\rm d}y,

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{1,4}

\displaystyle=\delta\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\partial_{y}\varepsilon)^{2}+\delta\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left((\varepsilon\sqrt{\phi_{1}})^{2}-5S^{4}(\varepsilon\sqrt{\phi_{1}})^{2}\right){\rm d}y,

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{1,5}

\displaystyle=(1-\delta)\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\partial_{y}\varepsilon)^{2}(1-\phi_{1}){\rm d}y-(1-\delta)\int_{\mathbb{R}}\varepsilon^{2}\frac{(\partial_{y}\phi_{1})^{2}}{4\phi_{1}}{\rm d}y.

Estimates on
\mathcal{F}_{1,1}
and
\mathcal{F}_{1,2}
.
We claim that

\mathcal{F}_{1,1}+\mathcal{F}_{1,2}=O\left(s^{-\frac{13}{4}}\right).

(3.42)

Indeed, from Lemma
3.3
and the bootstrap assumption (
3.13
), we directly have

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}}(S^{4}-V^{4})\varepsilon^{2}{\rm d}y\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\|R\phi\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|U\|_{L^{\infty}}\right)\mathcal{N}_{B}^{2}(\varepsilon)

\displaystyle+\left(\|R\phi\|^{4}_{L^{\infty}}+\|U\|^{4}_{L^{\infty}}\right)\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\lesssim s^{-4}.

Then, using the exponential decay of
Q
and the definition of
\phi_{1}
in (
3.12
),

\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}}S^{4}\varepsilon^{2}(1-\phi_{1}){\rm d}y\right|\lesssim\|S^{4}(1-\phi_{1})\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\lesssim s^{-4}.

Based on a similar argument as above, we deduce that

\left|\mathcal{F}_{1,2}\right|\lesssim\left(\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{\infty}}^{4}\right)\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\lesssim s^{-\frac{13}{4}}.

We see that (
3.42
) follows directly from the above estimates.

Estimate on
\mathcal{F}_{1,3}
.
We claim that

\mathcal{F}_{1,3}\geq\frac{\nu}{2}\left\|\varepsilon\sqrt{\phi_{1}}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+O\left(s^{-4}\right).

(3.43)

Indeed, from the orthogonality condition (
3.4
), we find

\displaystyle(\varepsilon\sqrt{\phi_{1}},Q_{1})^{2}+(\varepsilon\sqrt{\phi_{1}},\partial_{y}Q_{1})^{2}+(\varepsilon\sqrt{\phi_{1}},\Lambda Q)^{2}

\displaystyle=O\left(s^{-4}\right),

\displaystyle(\varepsilon\sqrt{\phi_{1}},Q_{2})^{2}+(\varepsilon\sqrt{\phi_{1}},\partial_{y}Q_{2})^{2}+(\varepsilon\sqrt{\phi_{1}},\Gamma(\Lambda Q))^{2}

\displaystyle=O\left(s^{-4}\right).

It follows from Proposition
2.1
and an standard localization argument that

\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left((\partial_{y}(\varepsilon\sqrt{\phi_{1}}))^{2}+(\varepsilon\sqrt{\phi_{1}})^{2}-5(Q_{1}^{4}+Q_{2}^{4})(\varepsilon\sqrt{\phi_{1}})^{2}\right){\rm d}y\geq\nu\left\|\varepsilon\sqrt{\phi_{1}}\right\|^{2}_{H^{1}}+O(s^{-4}).

We see that (
3.43
) follows from the above estimates and Lemma
2.9
.

Estimate on
\mathcal{F}_{1,4}
.
It holds

\mathcal{F}_{1,4}=\delta\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\partial_{y}\varepsilon)^{2}{\rm d}y+O(\delta)\int_{\mathbb{R}}\varepsilon^{2}\phi_{1}{\rm d}y.

(3.44)

Estimate on
\mathcal{F}_{1,5}
.
We claim that

\mathcal{F}_{1,5}=O\left(B^{-1}\|\varepsilon\sqrt{\phi_{1}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right).

(3.45)

Indeed, from the definition of
\phi_{1}
in (
3.12
), we have
1-\phi_{1}\geq 0
and

\displaystyle|\partial_{y}\phi_{1}|\lesssim B^{-1}\left|\chi_{1}^{\prime}\left(\frac{y}{B}-s^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)\right|\lesssim B^{-1}\phi_{1},

\displaystyle|\partial^{2}_{y}\phi_{1}|\lesssim B^{-2}\left|\chi_{1}^{\prime\prime}\left(\frac{y}{B}-s^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)\right|\lesssim B^{-2}\phi_{1},

which directly completes the proof for (
3.45
).

On the other hand, using (
3.28
) and (
3.33
), we find

|\mathcal{J}_{1}|+|\mathcal{J}_{2}|\lesssim\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}(|\rho_{1}|+|\rho_{2}|){\rm d}y\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{N}_{B}(\varepsilon)\lesssim(\log^{\frac{1}{2}}s)\mathcal{N}_{B}(\varepsilon).

(3.46)

Then, from Lemma
2.6
and the definition of
\phi_{1}
in (
3.12
),

\displaystyle|(\varepsilon\phi_{1},X_{1}\phi)|

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{N}_{B}(\varepsilon)\left(\int_{-\infty}^{0}y^{4}e^{-2|y|}{\rm d}y+\int_{0}^{B(s^{\frac{1}{2}}+2)}1{\rm d}y\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

(3.47)

\displaystyle+\mathcal{N}_{B}(\varepsilon)\left(\int_{B(s^{\frac{1}{2}}+2)}^{\infty}\phi_{1}{\rm d}y\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim\left(1+s^{\frac{1}{4}}\right)\mathcal{N}_{B}(\varepsilon).

Based on the above estimate and the bootstrap assumption (
3.13
), we obtain

|\mathcal{F}_{2}|\lesssim|\mu|\mathcal{N}_{B}^{2}(\varepsilon)+|b_{1}|(|\mathcal{J}_{1}|+|\mathcal{J}_{2}|)|(\varepsilon\phi_{1},X_{1}\phi)|\lesssim s^{-\frac{7}{2}}.

(3.48)

Here, we use the fact that

\phi_{2}\lesssim\phi_{1}\Longrightarrow\int_{\mathbb{R}}\varepsilon^{2}\phi_{2}{\rm d}y\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}}\varepsilon^{2}\phi_{1}{\rm d}y\lesssim\mathcal{N}_{B}^{2}(\varepsilon).

Combining the estimates (
3.42
)–(
3.48
), we complete the proof for (
3.40
).

Proof of (ii). From now on, we use the following notation:

f\simeq g\Longrightarrow f=g+O\left(\frac{C_{0}^{2}}{s^{4}\log^{\frac{5}{2}}s}+\frac{C_{0}}{s^{4}\log^{2}s}\right).

Step 1.
Estimate for the time variation of
\mathcal{F}_{1}
. We claim that

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm d}\mathcal{F}_{1}}{{\rm d}s}

\displaystyle\geq\dot{\Theta}(\varepsilon\phi_{1},X_{1}\phi)-\frac{5}{4}b_{1}\dot{\mu}(\varepsilon\phi_{1},X_{1}\phi)-\frac{1}{8}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{y}\phi_{1}{\rm d}y

(3.49)

\displaystyle+20\mu\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\partial_{y}Q_{2})(Q_{2}^{3}\varepsilon^{2}){\rm d}y+O\left(\frac{C_{0}^{2}}{s^{4}\log^{\frac{5}{2}}s}+\frac{C_{0}}{s^{4}\log^{2}s}\right).

By an elementary computation, we decompose

\frac{{\rm d}\mathcal{F}_{1}}{{\rm d}s}=\mathcal{I}_{1}+\mathcal{I}_{2},

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{1}

\displaystyle=2\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\partial_{s}\varepsilon)\left(-\partial_{y}^{2}\varepsilon+\varepsilon\phi_{1}-(V+\varepsilon)^{5}+V^{5}\right),

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{2}

\displaystyle=-2\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\partial_{s}V)\left((V+\varepsilon)^{5}-V^{5}-5V^{4}\varepsilon\right)+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{s}\phi_{1}{\rm d}y.

Estimate on
\mathcal{I}_{1}
.

We claim that

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{1}

\displaystyle\geq\dot{\Theta}(\varepsilon\phi_{1},X_{1}\phi)-\frac{5}{4}b_{1}\dot{\mu}(\varepsilon\phi_{1},X_{1}\phi)-\frac{1}{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{y}\phi_{1}{\rm d}y

(3.50)

\displaystyle+20z\frac{\dot{\lambda}}{\lambda}\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\partial_{y}Q_{2})(Q_{2}^{3}\varepsilon^{2}){\rm d}y+O\left(\frac{C_{0}^{2}}{s^{4}\log^{\frac{5}{2}}s}+\frac{C_{0}}{s^{4}\log^{2}s}\right).

Indeed, using Lemma
3.4
, we decompose

\mathcal{I}_{1}=\mathcal{I}_{1,1}+\mathcal{I}_{1,2}+\mathcal{I}_{1,3}+\mathcal{I}_{1,4}+\mathcal{I}_{1,5},

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{1,1}

\displaystyle=2\int_{\mathbb{R}}\bar{\Psi}(V)\left(\partial_{y}^{2}\varepsilon-\varepsilon\phi_{1}+(V+\varepsilon)^{5}-V^{5}\right){\rm d}y,

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{1,2}

\displaystyle=2\frac{\dot{\lambda}}{\lambda}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Lambda\varepsilon\left(-\partial_{y}^{2}\varepsilon+\varepsilon\phi_{1}-(V+\varepsilon)^{5}+V^{5}\right){\rm d}y,

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{1,3}

\displaystyle=2\int_{\mathbb{R}}\varepsilon(1-\phi_{1})\partial_{y}\left(\partial_{y}^{2}\varepsilon-\varepsilon+(V+\varepsilon)^{5}-V^{5}\right){\rm d}y,

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{1,4}

\displaystyle=2\Big(\frac{\dot{x}_{1}}{\lambda}-1\Big)\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{y}V\left(-\partial_{y}^{2}\varepsilon+\varepsilon\phi_{1}-(V+\varepsilon)^{5}+V^{5}\right){\rm d}y,

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{1,5}

\displaystyle=2\Big(\frac{\dot{x}_{1}}{\lambda}-1\Big)\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{y}\varepsilon\left(-\partial_{y}^{2}\varepsilon+\varepsilon\phi_{1}-(V+\varepsilon)^{5}+V^{5}\right){\rm d}y.

Here, we denote

\bar{\Psi}(V)=\Psi(V)-\left(\frac{\dot{\lambda}}{\lambda}+b_{1}\right)\Lambda V.

Estimate on
\mathcal{I}_{1,1}
.

We claim that

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{1,1}\simeq\dot{\Theta}(\varepsilon\phi_{1},X_{1}\phi)-\frac{5}{4}b_{1}\dot{\mu}(\varepsilon\phi_{1},X_{1}\phi).

(3.51)

Indeed, we decompose

\displaystyle\partial_{y}^{2}\varepsilon-\varepsilon\phi_{1}+(V+\varepsilon)^{5}-V^{5}

\displaystyle=\partial_{y}^{2}\varepsilon-\varepsilon+5(Q_{1}^{4}+Q_{2}^{4})\varepsilon

\displaystyle+(1-\phi_{1})\varepsilon+5(V^{4}-Q_{1}^{4}-Q_{2}^{4})\varepsilon

\displaystyle+10V^{3}\varepsilon^{2}+10V^{2}\varepsilon^{3}+5V\varepsilon^{4}+\varepsilon^{5}.

Based on the above identity and
{\rm{Ker}}\mathcal{L}={\rm{Span}}\{Q^{\prime}\}
, we compute

\displaystyle(\dot{z}-b_{1}z-\mu)\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{y}Q_{2}\left(\partial_{y}^{2}\varepsilon-\varepsilon\phi_{1}+(V+\varepsilon)^{5}-V^{5}\right){\rm d}y

\displaystyle=5(\dot{z}-b_{1}z-\mu)\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{y}Q_{2}(V^{4}-Q^{4}_{1}-Q^{4}_{2})\varepsilon{\rm d}y

\displaystyle+O\left(|\dot{z}-b_{1}z-\mu|\left(\|Q_{1}Q_{2}^{3}\|_{L^{2}}\mathcal{N}_{B}(\varepsilon)+\mathcal{N}_{B}^{2}(\varepsilon)\right)\right)

\displaystyle+O\left(|\dot{z}-b_{1}z-\mu|\left(\|Q_{1}^{3}\partial_{y}Q_{2}\|_{L^{2}}+\||\partial_{y}Q_{2}|^{\frac{1}{2}}(1-\phi_{1})\|_{L^{2}}\right)\mathcal{N}_{B}(\varepsilon)\right).

It follows from (
3.13
) and Lemma
3.5
that

\displaystyle(\dot{z}-b_{1}z-\mu)\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{y}Q_{2}\left(\partial_{y}^{2}\varepsilon-\varepsilon\phi_{1}+(V+\varepsilon)^{5}-V^{5}\right){\rm d}y

(3.52)

\displaystyle\simeq 5(\dot{z}-b_{1}z-\mu)\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{y}Q_{2}(V^{4}-Q^{4}_{1}-Q^{4}_{2})\varepsilon{\rm d}y.

Then, from the bootstrap assumption (
3.13
) and Lemma
3.3
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}M_{2Q}\left(\partial_{y}^{2}\varepsilon-\varepsilon\phi_{1}+(V+\varepsilon)^{5}-V^{5}\right){\rm d}y

\displaystyle=-\left(\varepsilon\phi_{1},M_{2Q}\right)+O\left(\|M_{2Q}\|_{L^{\infty}}\left(\|R\phi\|_{L^{\infty}}^{4}+\|U\|_{L^{\infty}}^{4}\right)\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}}+\mathcal{N}_{B}(\varepsilon)\right)

\displaystyle+O\left(\|M_{2Q}\|_{\dot{H}^{1}}\mathcal{N}_{B}(\varepsilon)+\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{\infty}}^{3}\|\varepsilon\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\right)=-\left(\varepsilon\phi_{1},M_{2Q}\right)+O\left(\frac{C_{0}}{s^{\frac{3}{2}}\log s}\right),

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}M_{3Q}\left(\partial_{y}^{2}\varepsilon-\varepsilon\phi_{1}+(V+\varepsilon)^{5}-V^{5}\right){\rm d}y

\displaystyle=-\left(\varepsilon\phi_{1},M_{3Q}\right)+O\left(\|M_{3Q}\|_{L^{\infty}}\left(\|R\phi\|_{L^{\infty}}^{4}+\|U\|_{L^{\infty}}^{4}\right)\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}}+\mathcal{N}_{B}(\varepsilon)\right)

\displaystyle+O\left(\|M_{3Q}\|_{\dot{H}^{1}}\mathcal{N}_{B}(\varepsilon)+\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{\infty}}^{3}\|\varepsilon\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\right)=-\left(\varepsilon\phi_{1},M_{3Q}\right)+O\left(\frac{C_{0}}{s^{\frac{3}{2}}\log s}\right).

Based on the above estimates and Lemma
3.5
, we find

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\vec{N}\cdot(M_{2Q},M_{3Q})\left(\partial_{y}^{2}\varepsilon-\varepsilon\phi_{1}+(V+\varepsilon)^{5}-V^{5}\right){\rm d}y

\displaystyle\simeq(\dot{\zeta}_{2}-\dot{b}_{1}+\alpha\mu e^{-z})\left(\varepsilon\phi_{1},X_{1}\phi\right)+(\dot{\zeta}_{2}+\alpha(1+\mu)e^{-z}+2b_{1}^{2})\left(\varepsilon\phi_{1},(X_{2}-X_{1})\phi\right).

Note that, from
b_{2}=(1+\mu)^{\frac{3}{2}}\zeta_{2}
and Lemma
3.5
, we compute

\dot{\zeta}_{2}-\dot{b}_{1}+\alpha\mu e^{-z}=\dot{\Theta}-\frac{3}{2}b_{1}\dot{\mu}+O\left(\frac{1}{s^{3}\log s}\right).

Combining the above estimates with Lemma
2.16
and Lemma
3.5
, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\vec{N}\cdot(M_{2Q},M_{3Q})\left(\partial_{y}^{2}\varepsilon-\varepsilon\phi_{1}+(V+\varepsilon)^{5}-V^{5}\right){\rm d}y

(3.53)

\displaystyle\simeq\dot{\Theta}(\varepsilon\phi_{1},X_{1}\phi)-\frac{3}{2}b_{1}\dot{\mu}(\varepsilon\phi_{1},X_{1}\phi).

Similar as above, from
\mathcal{L}\Lambda Q=-2Q
,
(\varepsilon,Q_{2})=(\varepsilon,\Gamma(\Lambda Q))=0
and (i) of Lemma
2.16
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\dot{\mu}\int_{\mathbb{R}}M_{4Q}\left(\partial_{y}^{2}\varepsilon-\varepsilon\phi_{1}+(V+\varepsilon)^{5}-V^{5}\right){\rm d}y\simeq\frac{1}{4}b_{1}\dot{\mu}(\varepsilon\phi_{1},X_{1}\phi).

(3.54)

Here, we use the fact that

\left(\varepsilon\phi_{1},\Gamma(\Lambda B)\phi\right)\lesssim\mathcal{N}_{B}(\varepsilon)\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}|\Gamma(\Lambda B)|^{2}\phi_{1}{\rm d}y\right)\lesssim\frac{C_{0}}{s^{\frac{3}{4}}\log s}.

On the other hand, from Lemma
3.3
and Lemma
3.6
, we find

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Psi_{2}(V)\left(\partial_{y}^{2}\varepsilon-\varepsilon\phi_{1}+(V+\varepsilon)^{5}-V^{5}\right){\rm d}y\right|

(3.55)

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\|\Psi_{2}(V)\|_{\dot{H}^{1}}+\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}|\Psi_{2}(V)|^{2}\phi_{1}{\rm d}y\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)\mathcal{N}_{B}(\varepsilon)

\displaystyle+\|\Psi_{2}(V)\|_{L^{\infty}}\left(\|R\phi\|_{L^{\infty}}^{3}+\|U\|_{L^{\infty}}^{3}+\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{\infty}}^{3}\right)\|\varepsilon\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\lesssim\frac{C_{0}}{s^{4}\log^{2}s}.

Similarly, we also find

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Psi_{3}(V)\left(\partial_{y}^{2}\varepsilon-\varepsilon\phi_{1}+(V+\varepsilon)^{5}-V^{5}\right){\rm d}y\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{C_{0}}{s^{4}\log^{2}s},

(3.56)

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Psi_{4}(V)\left(\partial_{y}^{2}\varepsilon-\varepsilon\phi_{1}+(V+\varepsilon)^{5}-V^{5}\right){\rm d}y\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{C_{0}}{s^{4}\log^{2}s}.

In particular, we find

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Psi_{1}(V)\left(\partial_{y}^{2}\varepsilon-\varepsilon\phi_{1}+(V+\varepsilon)^{5}-V^{5}\right){\rm d}y

(3.57)

\displaystyle\simeq 10m_{0}b_{1}(\dot{z}-b_{1}z-\mu)\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{y}Y_{2}\left(\partial_{y}^{2}\varepsilon-\varepsilon\phi_{1}+(V+\varepsilon)^{5}-V^{5}\right){\rm d}y

\displaystyle+\zeta_{2}(\dot{z}-b_{1}z-\mu)\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{y}X_{2}\left(\partial_{y}^{2}\varepsilon-\varepsilon\phi_{1}+(V+\varepsilon)^{5}-V^{5}\right){\rm d}y.

Last, from the definition of
V
in (
2.28
), we rewrite

\displaystyle\Lambda V

\displaystyle=\Lambda Q-\Gamma\Lambda Q-z\partial_{y}Q_{2}+b_{1}(\Lambda X)\phi+b_{1}yX\partial_{y}\phi

\displaystyle-\zeta_{2}\Gamma(\Lambda X)\phi-\zeta_{2}yX_{2}\partial_{y}\phi-z\zeta_{2}(\partial_{y}X_{2})\phi+e^{-z}\Lambda((A_{1}+B_{2})\phi)

\displaystyle-\frac{10m_{0}b_{1}\Gamma(\Lambda Y)}{(1+\mu)^{\frac{1}{4}}}-\frac{10m_{0}b_{1}z\partial_{y}Y_{2}}{(1+\mu)^{\frac{1}{4}}}+5(2\alpha m_{0}z+a_{0})e^{-z}\Lambda Y_{2}+\Lambda U_{1}.

Note that, from the bootstrap assumption (
3.13
) and Lemma
2.16
,

\displaystyle b_{1}(\Lambda X)\phi-\zeta_{2}(\Gamma(\Lambda X))\phi

\displaystyle=O_{H^{1}}\left(\frac{1}{s\log^{\frac{1}{2}}s}\right),

\displaystyle b_{1}yX_{1}\partial_{y}\phi-\zeta_{2}yX_{2}\partial_{y}\phi

\displaystyle=O_{H^{1}}\left(\frac{1}{s\log^{\frac{1}{2}}s}\right).

It follows from Lemma
2.16
that

\displaystyle\Lambda V

\displaystyle=\Lambda Q-\Gamma\Lambda Q-z\partial_{y}Q_{2}-z\zeta_{2}(\partial_{y}X_{2})\phi

(3.58)

\displaystyle-10m_{0}b_{1}z\partial_{y}Y_{2}+O_{H^{1}}\left(\frac{1}{s\log^{\frac{1}{2}}s}\right).

Based on the above estimate and Lemma
3.5
, we obtain

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\dot{\lambda}}{\lambda}+b_{1}\right)\Lambda V

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\dot{\lambda}}{\lambda}+b_{1}\right)\Lambda Q-\left(\frac{\dot{\lambda}}{\lambda}+b_{1}\right)\Gamma\Lambda Q

\displaystyle-z\left(\frac{\dot{\lambda}}{\lambda}+b_{1}\right)\partial_{y}Q_{2}-z\zeta_{2}\left(\frac{\dot{\lambda}}{\lambda}+b_{1}\right)(\partial_{y}X_{2})\phi

\displaystyle-10m_{0}b_{1}z\left(\frac{\dot{\lambda}}{\lambda}+b_{1}\right)\partial_{y}Y_{2}+O_{H^{1}}\left(\frac{C_{0}}{s^{\frac{5}{2}}\log^{\frac{3}{2}}s}\right).

Therefore, using a similar argument as in the proof for (
3.52
) and (
3.55
),

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\dot{\lambda}}{\lambda}+b_{1}\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Lambda V\left(\partial_{y}^{2}\varepsilon-\varepsilon\phi_{1}+(V+\varepsilon)^{5}-V^{5}\right){\rm d}y

(3.59)

\displaystyle\simeq-5z\left(\frac{\dot{\lambda}}{\lambda}+b_{1}\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{y}Q_{2}\left(V^{4}-Q_{1}^{4}-Q_{2}^{4}\right)\varepsilon{\rm d}y

\displaystyle-z\zeta_{2}\left(\frac{\dot{\lambda}}{\lambda}+b_{1}\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{y}X_{2}\left(\partial_{y}^{2}\varepsilon-\varepsilon\phi_{1}+(V+\varepsilon)^{5}-V^{5}\right){\rm d}y

\displaystyle-10m_{0}b_{1}z\left(\frac{\dot{\lambda}}{\lambda}+b_{1}\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{y}Y_{2}\left(\partial_{y}^{2}\varepsilon-\varepsilon\phi_{1}+(V+\varepsilon)^{5}-V^{5}\right){\rm d}y.

Combining the estimates (
3.52
)–(
3.57
) with (
3.59
), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{1,1}

\displaystyle\simeq\dot{\Theta}(\varepsilon\phi_{1},X_{1}\phi)-\frac{5}{4}b_{1}\dot{\mu}(\varepsilon\phi_{1},X_{1}\phi)

\displaystyle+5\mathcal{Z}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{y}Q_{2}(V^{4}-Q^{4}_{1}-Q^{4}_{2})\varepsilon{\rm d}y

\displaystyle+\zeta_{2}\mathcal{Z}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{y}X_{2}\left(\partial_{y}^{2}\varepsilon-\varepsilon\phi_{1}+(V+\varepsilon)^{5}-V^{5}\right){\rm d}y

\displaystyle+10m_{0}b_{1}\mathcal{Z}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{y}Y_{2}\left(\partial_{y}^{2}\varepsilon-\varepsilon\phi_{1}+(V+\varepsilon)^{5}-V^{5}\right){\rm d}y.

Here, we denote

\mathcal{Z}=\dot{z}-b_{1}z-\mu+z\left(\frac{\dot{\lambda}}{\lambda}+b_{1}\right).

In addition, from the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we directly have

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{y}Q_{2}(V^{4}-Q^{4}_{1}-Q^{4}_{2})\varepsilon{\rm d}y\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{N}_{B}(\varepsilon)\lesssim\frac{C_{0}}{s^{\frac{3}{2}}\log s},

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{y}X_{2}\left(\partial_{y}^{2}\varepsilon-\varepsilon\phi_{1}+(V+\varepsilon)^{5}-V^{5}\right){\rm d}y\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{N}_{B}(\varepsilon)\lesssim\frac{C_{0}}{s^{\frac{3}{2}}\log s},

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{y}Y_{2}\left(\partial_{y}^{2}\varepsilon-\varepsilon\phi_{1}+(V+\varepsilon)^{5}-V^{5}\right){\rm d}y\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{N}_{B}(\varepsilon)\lesssim\frac{C_{0}}{s^{\frac{3}{2}}\log s}.

We see that (
3.51
) follows directly from the above estimate and Lemma
3.5
.

Estimate on
\mathcal{I}_{1,2}
.
We claim that

\mathcal{I}_{1,2}\simeq 20z\frac{\dot{\lambda}}{\lambda}\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\partial_{y}Q_{2})(Q_{2}^{3}\varepsilon^{2}){\rm d}y+O\left(s^{-\frac{1}{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{y}\phi_{1}{\rm d}y\right).

(3.60)

Indeed, from integration by parts, we find

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Lambda\varepsilon(-\partial_{y}^{2}\varepsilon+\varepsilon\phi_{1}){\rm d}y

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\partial_{y}\varepsilon)^{2}{\rm d}y-\frac{B}{2}s^{\frac{1}{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{y}\phi_{1}{\rm d}y

\displaystyle-\frac{B}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\varepsilon^{2}\left(\frac{y}{B}-s^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)\partial_{y}\phi_{1}{\rm d}y.

Note that, from the definition of
\phi_{1}
in (
3.12
),

\left|\left(\frac{y}{B}-s^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)\partial_{y}\phi_{1}\right|\lesssim|\partial_{y}\phi_{1}|^{\frac{99}{100}}.

It follows from Lemma
3.5
and Hölder inequality that

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\dot{\lambda}}{\lambda}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\varepsilon^{2}\left(\frac{y}{B}-s^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)\partial_{y}\phi_{1}{\rm d}y\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\varepsilon^{2}|\partial_{y}\phi_{1}|{\rm d}y\right)^{\frac{99}{100}}\left(\frac{\|\varepsilon\|^{\frac{1}{50}}_{L^{2}}}{s\log s}\right)\lesssim s^{-\frac{1}{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\varepsilon^{2}|\partial_{y}\phi_{1}|{\rm d}y+s^{-10}.

Combining the above estimates with Lemma
3.5
, we find

\displaystyle 2\frac{\dot{\lambda}}{\lambda}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Lambda\varepsilon(-\partial_{y}^{2}\varepsilon+\varepsilon\phi_{1}){\rm d}y

\displaystyle=O\left(s^{-\frac{1}{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\varepsilon^{2}|\partial_{y}\phi_{1}|{\rm d}y\right)

\displaystyle+O\left(\frac{C_{0}^{2}}{s^{4}\log^{3}s}+\frac{1}{s^{10}}\right).

On the other hand, we decompose

\displaystyle(V+\varepsilon)^{5}-V^{5}

\displaystyle=5(Q_{1}^{4}+Q_{2}^{4})\varepsilon+5(V^{4}-Q_{1}^{4}-Q_{2}^{4})\varepsilon

\displaystyle+10V^{3}\varepsilon^{2}+5V^{2}\varepsilon^{3}+10V\varepsilon^{4}+\varepsilon^{5}.

Using integration by parts, we directly have

\displaystyle-2\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Lambda\varepsilon\left(5(Q_{1}^{4}+Q_{2}^{4})\varepsilon\right){\rm d}y

\displaystyle=20z\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\partial_{y}Q_{2})\left(Q_{2}^{3}\varepsilon^{2}\right){\rm d}y

\displaystyle+20\int_{\mathbb{R}}(y\partial_{y}Q_{1})(Q_{1}^{3}\varepsilon^{2}){\rm d}y

\displaystyle+20\int_{\mathbb{R}}((y-z)\partial_{y}Q_{2})(Q_{2}^{3}\varepsilon^{2}){\rm d}y,

which implies that

-2\frac{\dot{\lambda}}{\lambda}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Lambda\varepsilon\left(5(Q_{1}^{4}+Q_{2}^{4})\varepsilon\right){\rm d}y\simeq 20z\frac{\dot{\lambda}}{\lambda}\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\partial_{y}Q_{2})\left(Q_{2}^{3}\varepsilon^{2}\right){\rm d}y.

Then, using an elementary computation, we have

V^{4}-Q_{1}^{4}-Q_{2}^{4}=\left(S^{4}-Q_{1}^{4}-Q_{2}^{4}\right)+O\left(|S|^{3}|R\phi+U|+|R\phi+U|^{4}\right),

which implies that

\displaystyle\bigg|\frac{\dot{\lambda}}{\lambda}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Lambda\varepsilon\left(5(V^{4}-Q_{1}^{4}-Q_{2}^{4})\varepsilon\right){\rm d}y\bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{s\log s}\left(\|yQ_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|yQ_{2}\|_{L^{\infty}}\right)\|R\phi+U\|_{L^{\infty}}\mathcal{N}_{B}^{2}(\varepsilon)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{s\log s}\left(\mathcal{N}_{B}^{2}(\varepsilon)+\|\langle y\rangle(R\phi+U)^{4}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)\lesssim\frac{C_{0}^{2}}{s^{4}\log^{3}s}.

Based on a similar argument, we also have

\displaystyle\bigg|\frac{\dot{\lambda}}{\lambda}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Lambda\varepsilon\left(10V^{3}\varepsilon^{2}+5V^{2}\varepsilon^{3}+10V\varepsilon^{4}+\varepsilon^{5}\right){\rm d}y\bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\mathcal{N}_{B}^{2}\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{\infty}}}{s\log s}\left(1+\|yQ_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|yQ_{2}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{\mathcal{N}_{B}(\varepsilon)\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{\infty}}}{s\log s}\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}}\left(\|y(R\phi+U)^{3}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\|yV\|_{L^{\infty}}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{\infty}}}{s\log s}\left(\|R\phi\|_{L^{\infty}}^{3}+\|U\|_{L^{\infty}}^{3}+\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{\infty}}^{3}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{s\log s}\|R\phi+U\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{\infty}}^{3}\|\varepsilon\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\lesssim\frac{C_{0}^{2}}{s^{4}\log^{3}s}.

We see that (
3.60
) follows directly from the above estimates.

Estimate on
\mathcal{I}_{1,3}
.
We claim that

\mathcal{I}_{1,3}\geq-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{y}\phi_{1}{\rm d}y+O\left(\frac{C_{0}^{2}}{s^{4}\log^{3}s}\right).

(3.61)

Indeed, from integration by parts, we compute

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{1,3}

\displaystyle=-3\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\partial_{y}\varepsilon)^{2}\partial_{y}\phi_{1}{\rm d}y-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{y}\phi_{1}{\rm d}y

\displaystyle-2\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{y}\varepsilon\left((V+\varepsilon)^{5}-V^{5}\right)(1-\phi_{1}){\rm d}y

\displaystyle+2\int_{\mathbb{R}}\varepsilon\left((V+\varepsilon)^{5}-V^{5}\right)\partial_{y}\phi_{1}{\rm d}y+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{y}^{3}\phi_{1}{\rm d}y.

First, from the definition of
\phi_{1}
in (
3.12
),

\displaystyle|\partial_{y}\phi_{1}|\lesssim s^{-2},\ \ \mbox{on}\ \left(-\infty,s^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow S^{4}|\partial_{y}\phi_{1}|\lesssim s^{-2}(Q_{1}+Q_{2}),

\displaystyle|1-\phi_{1}|\lesssim s^{-2},\ \ \mbox{on}\ \left(-\infty,s^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow S^{4}|1-\phi_{1}|\lesssim s^{-2}(Q_{1}+Q_{2}).

It follows from the bootstrap assumption (
3.13
) and Lemma
3.3
that

\displaystyle\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\varepsilon\left((V+\varepsilon)^{5}-V^{5}\right)\partial_{y}\phi_{1}{\rm d}y\bigg|

\displaystyle+\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{y}\varepsilon\left((V+\varepsilon)^{5}-V^{5}\right)(1-\phi_{1}){\rm d}y\bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim s^{-2}\mathcal{N}_{B}^{2}(\varepsilon)+\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\left(\|R\phi+U\|_{L^{\infty}}^{4}+\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{\infty}}^{4}\right)

\displaystyle+\mathcal{N}_{B}(\varepsilon)\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}}\left(\|R\phi+U\|_{L^{\infty}}^{4}+\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{\infty}}^{4}\right)\lesssim\frac{C_{0}^{2}}{s^{4}\log^{3}s}.

Second, using again the definition of
\phi_{1}
in (
3.12
),

|\partial_{y}^{3}\phi_{1}|\lesssim B^{-2}|\partial_{y}\phi_{1}|\Longrightarrow\int_{\mathbb{R}}\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{y}^{3}\phi_{1}{\rm d}y=O\left(B^{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\varepsilon^{2}|\partial_{y}\phi_{1}|{\rm d}y\right).

We see that (
3.61
) follows directly from the above estimates.

Estimates on
\mathcal{I}_{1,4}
and
\mathcal{I}_{1,5}
.

We claim that

\mathcal{I}_{1,4}+\mathcal{I}_{1,5}=O\left(\frac{C_{0}^{2}}{s^{4}\log^{3}s}\right).

(3.62)

Indeed, we decompose again

\displaystyle\partial_{y}^{2}\varepsilon-\varepsilon\phi_{1}+(V+\varepsilon)^{5}-V^{5}

\displaystyle=\partial_{y}^{2}\varepsilon-\varepsilon+5(Q_{1}^{4}+Q_{2}^{4})\varepsilon

\displaystyle+(1-\phi_{1})\varepsilon+5(V^{4}-Q_{1}^{4}-Q_{2}^{4})\varepsilon

\displaystyle+10V^{3}\varepsilon^{2}+10V^{2}\varepsilon^{3}+5V\varepsilon^{4}+\varepsilon^{5}.

Based on the above identity and
{\rm{Ker}}\mathcal{L}={\rm{Span}}\{Q^{\prime}\}
, we compute

\displaystyle\Big|\Big(\frac{\dot{x}_{1}}{\lambda}-1\Big)\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{y}S\left(-\partial_{y}^{2}\varepsilon+\varepsilon\phi_{1}-(V+\varepsilon)^{5}+V^{5}\right){\rm d}y\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{C_{0}}{s^{\frac{3}{2}}\log s}\left(\|R\phi+U\|_{L^{\infty}}\mathcal{N}_{B}(\varepsilon)+\|R\phi+U\|_{L^{\infty}}^{4}\mathcal{N}_{B}(\varepsilon)+\mathcal{N}_{B}^{2}(\varepsilon)\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{C_{0}}{s^{\frac{3}{2}}\log s}\left(\|\partial_{y}Q_{1}|^{\frac{1}{2}}(1-\phi_{1})\|_{L^{\infty}}+\||\partial_{y}Q_{2}|^{\frac{1}{2}}(1-\phi_{1})\|_{L^{2}}\right)\mathcal{N}_{B}(\varepsilon)

\displaystyle+\frac{C_{0}}{s^{\frac{3}{2}}\log s}\left(\|(\partial_{y}Q_{1})Q^{2}_{2}\|_{L^{2}}+\|(\partial_{y}Q_{2})Q^{2}_{1}\|_{L^{2}}\right)\mathcal{N}_{B}(\varepsilon)\lesssim\frac{C_{0}^{2}}{s^{4}\log^{3}s}.

On the other hand, using an argument similar to that in the proof for Lemma
2.16
,

\|\partial_{y}(R\phi)\|_{H^{1}}+\|\partial_{y}U\|_{H^{1}}\lesssim\|(\partial_{y}R)\phi\|_{H^{1}}+\|\partial_{y}U\|_{H^{1}}+\|R\partial_{y}\phi\|_{H^{1}}\lesssim\frac{1}{s\log s}.

It follows directly from the bootstrap assumption (
3.13
) and Lemma
3.3
that

\displaystyle\Big|\Big(\frac{\dot{x}_{1}}{\lambda}-1\Big)\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\partial_{y}(R\phi+U))\left(-\partial_{y}^{2}\varepsilon+\varepsilon\phi_{1}-(V+\varepsilon)^{5}+V^{5}\right){\rm d}y\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{C_{0}}{s^{\frac{3}{2}}\log s}\|\partial_{y}(R\phi+U)\|_{H^{1}}\left(\|R\phi+U\|_{L^{\infty}}^{4}+\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{\infty}}^{4}\right)\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+\frac{C_{0}}{s^{\frac{3}{2}}\log s}\|\partial_{y}(R\phi+U)\|_{H^{1}}\mathcal{N}_{B}(\varepsilon)\lesssim\frac{C_{0}^{2}}{s^{4}\log^{3}s}.

Similar as above, we also check that

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{I}_{1,5}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{C_{0}}{s^{\frac{3}{2}}\log s}\mathcal{N}_{B}(\varepsilon)\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}}\left(\|R\phi+U\|_{L^{\infty}}^{4}+\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{\infty}}^{4}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{C_{0}}{s^{\frac{3}{2}}\log s}\mathcal{N}_{B}^{2}(\varepsilon)\lesssim\frac{C_{0}^{3}}{s^{\frac{9s}{2}}\log^{3}s}+\frac{C^{2}_{0}}{s^{5}\log^{2}s}.

We see that (
3.62
) follows directly from the above estimates.

Combining (
3.51
), (
3.60
), (
3.61
) with (
3.62
), we complete the proof for (
3.50
).

Estimate on
\mathcal{I}_{2}
.

We claim that

\mathcal{I}_{2}\simeq 20\dot{z}\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\partial_{y}Q_{2})\left(Q_{2}^{3}\varepsilon^{2}\right){\rm d}y+O\left(\frac{B}{s^{\frac{1}{2}}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\varepsilon^{2}|\partial_{y}\phi_{1}|{\rm d}y\right).

(3.63)

Indeed, from the definition of
V
in (
2.28
), we compute
10
10

10

See the proof of (
2.38
)–(

) for more detail.

\displaystyle\partial_{s}V

\displaystyle=\dot{z}M_{1Q}+\dot{\zeta}_{2}M_{2Q}+\dot{b}_{1}{M_{3Q}}+\dot{\mu}M_{4Q}

(3.64)

\displaystyle+2\dot{b}_{1}b_{1}(E_{1}+F_{2})\phi-\dot{z}b_{1}^{2}\phi\partial_{y}F_{2}+R\partial_{s}\phi

\displaystyle+b_{1}^{2}(E_{1}+F_{2})\partial_{s}\phi+30m_{0}z\dot{b}_{1}b_{1}Y_{2}+\frac{\dot{\mu}b_{1}^{2}\phi}{2(1+\mu)}\Gamma(\Lambda F)

\displaystyle+\dot{z}\left(\zeta_{2}\phi\partial_{y}X_{2}+{10m_{0}b_{1}}\partial_{y}Y_{2}\right)+\frac{5}{2}m_{0}b_{1}\dot{\mu}\left(Y_{2}-2\Gamma(\Lambda Y)\right)

\displaystyle-10\alpha m_{0}\dot{z}ze^{-z}(Y_{2}+\partial_{y}Y_{2})-\dot{z}e^{-z}(A_{1}+B_{2})\phi-\dot{z}e^{-z}\phi\partial_{y}B_{2}.

It follows from (

) and Lemma

that

\|\partial_{s}V\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim s^{-1}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\partial_{s}V=\dot{z}\partial_{y}Q_{2}+O_{L^{\infty}}\left(\frac{1}{s\log s}\right).

(3.65)

On the one hand, using again (

) and Lemma

, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}|\partial_{s}V||(R\phi+U)|^{3}\varepsilon^{2}{\rm d}y

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\partial_{s}V\|_{L^{\infty}}\|R\phi+U\|_{L^{\infty}}\mathcal{N}_{B}^{2}(\varepsilon)\lesssim\frac{C_{0}^{2}}{s^{5}\log^{3}s}.

It follows from (

) and Lemma
2.9
that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\partial_{s}V)V^{3}\varepsilon^{2}{\rm d}y

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\partial_{s}V)S^{3}\varepsilon^{2}{\rm d}y+O\left(\frac{C_{0}^{2}}{s^{5}\log^{3}s}\right)

\displaystyle=-\dot{z}\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\partial_{y}Q_{2})\left(Q_{2}^{3}\varepsilon^{2}\right){\rm d}y+O\left(\frac{C_{0}^{2}}{s^{4}\log^{3}s}\right).

On the other hand, using again (
3.65
) and Lemma
3.3
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}|\partial_{s}V|\left(|V|^{2}|\varepsilon|^{3}+|V||\varepsilon|^{4}+|\varepsilon|^{5}\right){\rm d}y

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\partial_{s}V\|_{L^{\infty}}\left(\|V\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{\infty}}^{3}\right)\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\lesssim s^{-\frac{17}{4}}.

Last, using again the definition of
\phi_{1}
in (
3.12
),

|\partial_{s}\phi_{1}|\lesssim\frac{B}{s^{\frac{1}{2}}}|\partial_{y}\phi_{1}|\Longrightarrow\int_{\mathbb{R}}\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{s}\phi_{1}{\rm d}y=O\left(\frac{B}{s^{\frac{1}{2}}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\varepsilon^{2}|\partial_{y}\phi_{1}|{\rm d}y\right).

Combining the above estimates,
we directly complete the proof for (
3.63
).

Note that, from (
3.50
) and (
3.63
), we directly have

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm d}\mathcal{F}_{1}}{{\rm d}s}

\displaystyle\geq\dot{\Theta}(\varepsilon\phi_{1},X_{1}\phi)-\frac{5}{4}b_{2}\dot{\mu}(\varepsilon\phi_{1},X_{1}\phi)-\frac{1}{8}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{y}\phi_{1}{\rm d}y

\displaystyle+20\left(\dot{z}+z\frac{\dot{\lambda}}{\lambda}\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\partial_{y}Q_{2})(Q_{2}^{3}\varepsilon^{2}){\rm d}y+O\left(\frac{C_{0}^{2}}{s^{4}\log^{\frac{5}{2}}s}+\frac{C_{0}}{s^{4}\log^{2}s}\right).

Note also that, from Lemma
3.5
, we deduce that

20\left(\dot{z}+z\frac{\dot{\lambda}}{\lambda}\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\partial_{y}Q_{2})(Q_{2}^{3}\varepsilon^{2}){\rm d}y\simeq 20\mu\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\partial_{y}Q_{2})(Q_{2}^{3}\varepsilon^{2}){\rm d}y.

Combining the above two estimates, we complete the proof for (
3.49
).

Step 2.
Estimate for the time variation of
\mathcal{F}_{2}
. We claim that

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm d}\mathcal{F}_{2}}{{\rm d}s}

\displaystyle\simeq-20\mu\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\partial_{y}Q_{2})(Q_{2}^{3}\varepsilon^{2}){\rm d}y

(3.66)

\displaystyle-\dot{\Theta}(\varepsilon\phi_{1},X_{1}\phi)+\frac{5}{4}b_{1}\dot{\mu}(\varepsilon\phi_{1},X_{1}\phi).

Indeed, we decompose

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm d}\mathcal{F}_{2}}{{\rm d}s}

\displaystyle=\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}s}\left(\mu\int_{\mathbb{R}}\varepsilon^{2}\phi_{2}{\rm d}y\right)-\left(b_{1}\left(\mathcal{J}_{2}+\left(c_{0}+\frac{5}{4}\right)\mathcal{J}_{1}\right)\right)\left(\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}s}(\varepsilon\phi_{1},X_{1}\phi)\right)

\displaystyle-\left(\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}s}\left(b_{1}\left(\mathcal{J}_{2}+\left(c_{0}+\frac{5}{4}\right)\mathcal{J}_{1}\right)\right)\right)(\varepsilon\phi_{1},X_{1}\phi)=\mathcal{I}_{3}+\mathcal{I}_{4}+\mathcal{I}_{5}.

Estimate on
\mathcal{I}_{3}
.
We claim that

\mathcal{I}_{3}\simeq-20\mu\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\partial_{y}Q_{2})(Q_{2}^{3}\varepsilon^{2}){\rm d}y.

(3.67)

Indeed, by an elementary computation, we find

\mathcal{I}_{3}=2\mu\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\partial_{s}\varepsilon)\varepsilon\phi_{2}{\rm d}y+\mu\int_{\mathbb{R}}\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{s}\phi_{2}{\rm d}y+\dot{\mu}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\varepsilon^{2}\phi_{2}{\rm d}y.

First, from Lemma
3.4
, we compute

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\partial_{s}\varepsilon)\varepsilon\phi_{2}{\rm d}y

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\partial_{y}^{2}\varepsilon-\varepsilon+(V+\varepsilon)^{5}-V^{5}\right)(\partial_{y}\varepsilon)\phi_{2}{\rm d}y

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\partial_{y}^{2}\varepsilon-\varepsilon+(V+\varepsilon)^{5}-V^{5}\right)\varepsilon(\partial_{y}\phi_{2}){\rm d}y

\displaystyle+\bigg(\frac{\dot{\lambda}}{\lambda}+b_{1}\bigg)\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\Lambda V)\varepsilon\phi_{2}{\rm d}y+\left(\frac{\dot{x}_{1}}{\lambda}-1\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\partial_{y}V\right)\varepsilon\phi_{2}{\rm d}y

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Psi(V)\varepsilon\phi_{2}{\rm d}y+\frac{\dot{\lambda}}{\lambda}\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\Lambda\varepsilon)\varepsilon\phi_{2}{\rm d}y+\left(\frac{\dot{x}_{1}}{\lambda}-1\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\partial_{y}\varepsilon\right)\varepsilon\phi_{2}{\rm d}y.

Recall that, we decompose

\displaystyle\partial_{y}^{2}\varepsilon-\varepsilon+(V+\varepsilon)^{5}-V^{5}

\displaystyle=\partial_{y}^{2}\varepsilon-\varepsilon+5(Q_{1}^{4}+Q_{2}^{4})\varepsilon

\displaystyle+5(V^{4}-Q_{1}^{4}-Q_{2}^{4})\varepsilon+\varepsilon^{5}

\displaystyle+10V^{3}\varepsilon^{2}+10V^{2}\varepsilon^{3}+5V\varepsilon^{4}.

Using integration by parts and Lemma
2.9
, we check that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\partial_{y}^{2}\varepsilon-\varepsilon+5(Q_{1}^{4}+Q_{2}^{4})\varepsilon\right)(\partial_{y}\varepsilon)\phi_{2}{\rm d}y

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\partial_{y}\varepsilon)^{2}\partial_{y}\phi_{2}{\rm d}y+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{y}\phi_{2}{\rm d}y

\displaystyle-10\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\partial_{y}Q_{2})(Q_{2}^{3}\varepsilon^{2}){\rm d}y+O\left(\frac{C_{0}^{2}}{s^{4}\log^{2}s}\right).

Note that, from the definition of
\phi_{2}
in (
3.39
), we find

\partial_{y}\phi_{2}=\frac{1}{z}\chi^{\prime}_{2}\left(\frac{y}{z}\right)\Longrightarrow|\partial_{y}\phi_{2}|\lesssim\frac{\phi_{1}}{\log s}.

(3.68)

It follows from the bootstrap assumption (
3.13
) that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\partial_{y}^{2}\varepsilon-\varepsilon+5(Q_{1}^{4}+Q_{2}^{4})\varepsilon\right)(\partial_{y}\varepsilon)\phi_{2}{\rm d}y

\displaystyle=-10\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\partial_{y}Q_{2})(Q_{2}^{3}\varepsilon^{2}){\rm d}y+O\left(\frac{C_{0}^{2}}{s^{4}\log^{2}s}+\frac{C_{0}^{2}}{s^{3}\log^{3}s}\right).

Then, using again Lemma
3.3
and the bootstrap assumption (
3.13
),

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(|V^{4}-Q_{1}^{4}-Q_{2}^{4}||\varepsilon|+|\varepsilon|^{5}\right)|\partial_{y}\varepsilon|\phi_{2}{\rm d}y

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(|V|^{3}\varepsilon^{2}+V^{2}|\varepsilon|^{3}+|V|\varepsilon^{4}\right)|\partial_{y}\varepsilon|\phi_{2}{\rm d}y

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\|Q_{1}^{3}Q_{2}\|_{L^{2}}+\|Q_{1}Q^{3}_{2}\|_{L^{2}}\right)\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}}\mathcal{N}_{B}(\varepsilon)

\displaystyle+\left(\|R\phi+U\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{\infty}}\right)\mathcal{N}_{B}^{2}(\varepsilon)\lesssim\frac{C_{0}^{2}}{s^{4}\log^{2}s}.

Combining the above two estimates, we deduce that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\partial_{y}^{2}\varepsilon-\varepsilon+(V+\varepsilon)^{5}-V^{5}\right)(\partial_{y}\varepsilon)\phi_{2}{\rm d}y

(3.69)

\displaystyle=-10\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\partial_{y}Q_{2})(Q_{2}^{3}\varepsilon^{2}){\rm d}y+O\left(\frac{C_{0}^{2}}{s^{4}\log^{2}s}+\frac{C_{0}^{2}}{s^{3}\log^{3}s}\right).

Using again (
3.68
), the bootstrap assumption (
3.13
) and Lemma
3.3
,

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\partial_{y}^{2}\varepsilon-\varepsilon+(V+\varepsilon)^{5}-V^{5}\right)\varepsilon(\partial_{y}\phi_{2}){\rm d}y\right|

(3.70)

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(z^{-1}+\|R\phi+U\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{\infty}}\right)\mathcal{N}_{B}^{2}(\varepsilon)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{C_{0}^{2}}{s^{3}\log^{3}s}+\frac{C_{0}^{2}}{s^{4}\log^{2}s}+\frac{C_{0}^{2}}{s^{\frac{17}{4}}\log^{2}s}.

Then, from
(\varepsilon,\Lambda Q)=(\varepsilon,\Gamma(\Lambda Q))=(\varepsilon,\partial_{y}Q_{1})=(\varepsilon,\partial_{y}Q_{2})=0
and (
3.58
),

\displaystyle\bigg|\bigg(\frac{\dot{\lambda}}{\lambda}+b_{1}\bigg)\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\Lambda V)\varepsilon\phi_{2}{\rm d}y\bigg|\lesssim\frac{C_{0}^{2}}{s^{4}\log^{2}s},

(3.71)

\displaystyle\bigg|\left(\frac{\dot{x}_{1}}{\lambda}-1\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\partial_{y}V\right)\varepsilon\phi_{2}{\rm d}y\bigg|\lesssim\frac{C_{0}^{2}}{s^{4}\log^{2}s}.

Next, using again
(\varepsilon,\Gamma(\Lambda Q))=(\varepsilon,\partial_{y}Q_{2})=0
and Lemma
3.5
, we find

\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}}({\vec{{\rm{Mod}}}}\cdot\vec{M}Q)\varepsilon\phi_{2}{\rm d}y\bigg|\lesssim s^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{N}_{B}^{2}(\varepsilon)\lesssim\frac{C_{0}^{2}}{s^{\frac{7}{2}}\log^{2}s}.

In addition, from Lemma
3.5
, Lemma
3.6
and
\phi_{2}\lesssim\phi_{1}
,

\sum_{i=1}^{4}\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Psi_{i}({V})\varepsilon\phi_{2}{\rm d}y\bigg|\lesssim s^{-\frac{5}{2}}\mathcal{N}_{B}(\varepsilon)\lesssim\frac{C_{0}}{s^{4}\log s}.

Combining the above estimates with Definition
2.14
and Proposition
2.18
, we obtain

\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Psi({V})\varepsilon\phi_{2}{\rm d}y\bigg|\lesssim\frac{C_{0}^{2}}{s^{\frac{7}{2}}\log^{2}s}+\frac{C_{0}}{s^{4}\log s}.

(3.72)

By integration by parts, we compute

\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\Lambda\varepsilon)\varepsilon\phi_{2}{\rm d}y=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\varepsilon^{2}\phi_{2}{\rm d}y-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\partial_{y}\varepsilon)^{2}\phi_{2}{\rm d}y-\frac{1}{2}(\partial_{y}\varepsilon)^{2}y\partial_{y}\phi_{2}{\rm d}y,

which implies that

\displaystyle\bigg|\frac{\dot{\lambda}}{\lambda}\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\Lambda\varepsilon)\varepsilon\phi_{2}{\rm d}y\bigg|+\bigg|\left(\frac{\dot{x}_{1}}{\lambda}-1\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\partial_{y}\varepsilon\right)\varepsilon\phi_{2}{\rm d}y\bigg|

(3.73)

\displaystyle\lesssim s^{-1}\mathcal{N}_{B}^{2}(\varepsilon)+\mathcal{N}_{B}^{3}(\varepsilon)\lesssim\frac{C_{0}^{2}}{s^{4}\log^{2}s}+\frac{C_{0}^{3}}{s^{\frac{9}{2}}\log^{3}s}.

Last, from Lemma
3.5
, the definition of
\phi_{2}
in (
3.39
) and (
3.13
),

\bigg|\mu\int_{\mathbb{R}}\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{s}\phi_{2}{\rm d}y\bigg|+\bigg|\dot{\mu}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\varepsilon^{2}\phi_{2}{\rm d}y\bigg|\lesssim\frac{C_{0}^{2}}{s^{5}\log^{2}s}.

(3.74)

We see that (
3.67
) follows directly from (
3.69
)–(
3.74
).

Estimate on
\mathcal{I}_{4}
.
We claim that

\mathcal{I}_{4}=O\left(\frac{C_{0}^{2}}{s^{4}\log^{\frac{5}{2}}s}\right).

(3.75)

Indeed, we first decompose

\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}s}\left(\varepsilon\phi_{1},X_{1}\phi\right)=\left((\partial_{s}\varepsilon)\phi_{1},X_{1}\phi\right)+\left(\varepsilon(\partial_{s}\phi_{1}),X_{1}\phi\right)+\left(\varepsilon\phi_{1},X_{1}\partial_{s}\phi\right).

From Lemma
3.4
and integration by parts,

\displaystyle\left((\partial_{s}\varepsilon)\phi_{1},X_{1}\phi\right)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\partial_{y}^{2}\varepsilon-\varepsilon+(V+\varepsilon)^{5}-V^{5}\right)\left(\partial_{y}(X_{1}\phi\phi_{1})\right){\rm d}y

\displaystyle+\left(\frac{\dot{\lambda}}{\lambda}+b_{1}\right)\left((\Lambda V)\phi_{1},X_{1}\phi\right)+\left(\frac{\dot{x}_{1}}{\lambda}-1\right)\left((\partial_{y}V)\phi_{1},X_{1}\phi\right)

\displaystyle-(\Psi(V)\phi_{1},X_{1}\phi)+\frac{\dot{\lambda}}{\lambda}\left((\Lambda\varepsilon)\phi_{1},X_{1}\phi\right)+\left(\frac{\dot{x}_{1}}{\lambda}-1\right)\left((\partial_{y}\varepsilon)\phi_{1},X_{1}\phi\right).

By an elementary computation, we find

\partial_{y}(X\phi\phi_{1})=(\partial_{y}X)\phi\phi_{1}+X(\partial_{y}\phi)\phi_{1}+X\phi\partial_{y}\phi_{1},

which implies that

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\partial_{y}^{2}\varepsilon-\varepsilon\right)\left(\partial_{y}(X\phi\phi_{1})\right){\rm d}y\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{N}_{B}(\varepsilon)\left(\|X\partial_{y}\phi\|_{L^{2}}+\|\partial_{y}\phi_{1}\|_{L^{1}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)

\displaystyle+\mathcal{N}_{B}(\varepsilon)\left(1+\|\partial_{y}X\|_{L^{2}}\right)\lesssim\frac{C_{0}}{s^{\frac{3}{2}}\log s}.

Using a similar argument as above and Lemma
3.3
, we also find

\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left((V+\varepsilon)^{5}-V^{5}\right)\left(\partial_{y}(X\phi\phi_{1})\right){\rm d}y\right|\lesssim\frac{C_{0}}{s^{\frac{3}{2}}\log s}.

Next, from (
3.13
), (
3.58
) and Lemma
3.5
,

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\dot{\lambda}}{\lambda}+b_{1}\right)\left((\Lambda V)\phi_{1},X_{1}\phi\right)+\left(\frac{\dot{x}_{1}}{\lambda}-1\right)\left((\partial_{y}V)\phi_{1},X_{1}\phi\right)

\displaystyle=-z\left(\frac{\dot{\lambda}}{\lambda}+b_{1}\right)((\partial_{y}Q_{2}))\phi_{1},X\phi)+O\left(\frac{C_{0}}{s^{\frac{3}{2}}\log s}\right).

Similarly, from the definition of
\vec{\rm{Mod}}
and
\vec{M}Q
in (
2.31
)–(
2.32
),

\left((\vec{\rm{Mod}}\cdot\vec{M}Q)\phi_{1},X\phi\right)=(\dot{z}-b_{1}z-\mu)((\partial_{y}Q_{2}))\phi_{1},X\phi)+O\left(\frac{C_{0}}{s^{\frac{3}{2}}\log s}\right).

It follows from Lemma
3.6
and Proposition
2.18
that

-(\Psi(V)\phi_{1},X\phi)=-(\dot{z}-b_{1}z-\mu)((\partial_{y}Q_{2}))\phi_{1},X\phi)+O\left(\frac{C_{0}}{s^{\frac{3}{2}}\log s}\right).

Based on the above two estimates and Lemma
3.5
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\dot{\lambda}}{\lambda}+b_{1}\right)\left((\Lambda V)\phi_{1},X_{1}\phi\right)-(\Psi(V)\phi_{1},X\phi)

\displaystyle+\left(\frac{\dot{x}_{1}}{\lambda}-1\right)\left((\partial_{y}V)\phi_{1},X_{1}\phi\right)=O\left(\frac{C_{0}}{s^{\frac{3}{2}}\log s}\right).

Then, from Lemma
3.5
and the bootstrap assumption,

\displaystyle\bigg|\frac{\dot{\lambda}}{\lambda}\left((\Lambda\varepsilon)\phi_{1},X_{1}\phi\right)\bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\mathcal{N}_{B}(\varepsilon)}{s\log s}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}(1+y^{2})X_{1}^{2}\phi_{1}{\rm d}y\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim\frac{C_{0}}{s^{\frac{3}{2}}\log s},

\displaystyle\bigg|\left(\frac{\dot{x}_{1}}{\lambda}-1\right)\left((\partial_{y}\varepsilon)\phi_{1},X_{1}\phi\right)\bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{C_{0}\mathcal{N}_{B}(\varepsilon)}{s^{\frac{3}{2}}\log s}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}(1+y^{2})X_{1}^{2}\phi_{1}{\rm d}y\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim\frac{C_{0}}{s^{\frac{3}{2}}\log s}.

Here, we use the fact that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}(1+y^{2})X_{1}^{2}\phi_{1}{\rm d}y

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{-\infty}^{0}e^{-|y|}{\rm d}y+\int_{B(s^{\frac{1}{2}}+2)}^{\infty}\phi_{1}{\rm d}y

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{B(s^{\frac{1}{2}}+2)}(1+y^{2}){\rm d}y\lesssim s^{\frac{3}{2}}.

Last, from the definition of
\phi
and
\phi_{1}
in (
2.25
) and (
3.12
), we find

|\partial_{s}\phi|\lesssim s^{-1}\textbf{1}_{[\frac{s}{2},\frac{5s}{2}]}\quad\mbox{and}\quad|\partial_{s}\phi_{1}|\lesssim s^{-\frac{1}{2}}\phi_{1},

which directly implies that

\left|\left(\varepsilon(\partial_{s}\phi_{1}),X_{1}\phi\right)\right|+\left|\left(\varepsilon\phi_{1},X_{1}\partial_{s}\phi\right)\right|\lesssim\mathcal{N}_{B}(\varepsilon)\lesssim\frac{C_{0}}{s^{\frac{3}{2}}\log s}.

Combining the above estimates with (
3.46
), we complete the proof of (
3.75
).

Estimate on
\mathcal{I}_{5}
.

We claim that

\mathcal{I}_{5}\simeq-\dot{\Theta}(\varepsilon\phi_{1},X_{1}\phi)+\frac{5}{4}b_{1}\dot{\mu}(\varepsilon\phi_{1},X_{1}\phi).

(3.76)

Indeed, from Lemma
3.7
and Lemma
3.9
, we compute

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}s}\left(b_{1}\left(\mathcal{J}_{2}+\left(c_{0}+\frac{5}{4}\right)\mathcal{J}_{1}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\dot{\Theta}-\frac{5}{4}b_{1}\dot{\mu}+\frac{5}{2}\alpha\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}s}(ze^{-z})+2\Theta\left(c_{0}+\frac{5}{4}\right)b_{1}

\displaystyle+\dot{b}_{1}\left(\mathcal{J}_{2}+\left(c_{0}+\frac{5}{4}\right)\mathcal{J}_{1}\right)+O\left(\frac{1}{s^{3}\log s}\right).

It follows from Lemma
3.5
and (
3.46
) that

\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}s}\left(b_{1}\left(\mathcal{J}_{2}+\left(c_{0}+\frac{5}{4}\right)\mathcal{J}_{1}\right)\right)=\dot{\Theta}-\frac{5}{4}b_{1}\dot{\mu}+O\left(s^{-3}\right).

Combining the above estimate with (
3.47
), we complete the proof of (
3.76
).

We see that (
3.66
) follows directly from (
3.67
), (
3.75
) and (
3.76
).

Step 3.

Conclusion. Combining (
3.49
) and (
3.66
), we complete the proof of (
3.41
).
∎

3.5.
End of the proof for Proposition
3.2

We are in a position to complete the proof of Proposition
3.2
via bootstrap argument. We start by closing all bootstrap estimates except the distance and position parameters
(z,x_{1})
. Then, we prove the existence of suitable final data
(z^{in},x_{1}^{in})
using a topological argument.

Proof of Proposition
3.2
.

Step 1.
Closing the estimate for
\varepsilon
.
Integrating (
3.41
) on
[s,s^{in}]
for any
s\in[s^{*},s^{in}]
and then using
\varepsilon(s^{in})=0
, we have

\mathcal{F}(s)\lesssim\frac{C_{0}^{2}}{s^{3}\log^{\frac{5}{2}}s}+\frac{C_{0}}{s^{3}\log^{2}s}.

Thus, from (
3.40
), for
s_{0}>1
large enough (depending on
C_{0}
),

\mathcal{N}_{B}^{2}(\varepsilon)\lesssim\mathcal{F}+s^{-\frac{13}{4}}\lesssim\frac{C_{0}^{2}}{s^{3}\log^{\frac{5}{2}}s}+\frac{C_{0}}{s^{3}\log^{2}s}.

This strictly improves the estimate on
\varepsilon
in (
3.13
) for
C_{0}
large enough.

Step 2.
Closing the estimate for
\Theta
. Note that, from the bootstrap assumption (
3.13
) and (
3.14
), we find

\frac{z^{-1}}{3\alpha}e^{z}=s^{2}+O\left(\frac{s^{2}}{\log^{\frac{1}{2}}s}\right)\Longrightarrow\alpha e^{-z}=\frac{1}{6s^{2}\log s}+O\left(\frac{1}{s^{2}\log^{\frac{3}{2}}s}\right).

(3.77)

From Lemma
3.5
, the bootstrap assumption (
3.13
), (
3.31
), (
3.32
) and (
3.77
),

c_{0}b_{1}\dot{\mu}-b_{1}\dot{\mathcal{J}}_{2}=c_{0}\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}s}(b_{1}\mu)-\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}s}(b_{1}\mathcal{J}_{2})+O\left(\frac{C_{0}}{s^{\frac{7}{2}}\log^{\frac{3}{2}}s}\right).

Similarly, from (
3.13
) and (
3.31
)–(
3.32
), we find

|b_{1}\mu|+|b_{1}\mathcal{J}_{2}|\lesssim\frac{1}{s^{2}\log s}+\frac{C_{0}}{s^{\frac{5}{2}}\log^{\frac{3}{2}}s}.

Combining the above estimate with Lemma
3.9
and (
3.9
), we find

\Theta(s)=-\frac{5\alpha}{2}ze^{-z}+O\left(\frac{1}{s^{2}\log s}+\frac{C^{2}_{0}}{s^{\frac{5}{2}}\log^{\frac{3}{2}}s}\right).

(3.78)

It follows from (
3.14
) and (
3.77
) that

\Theta(s)=-\frac{5}{6s^{2}}+O\left(\frac{1}{s^{2}\log^{\frac{1}{2}}s}+\frac{C_{0}}{s^{\frac{5}{2}}\log^{\frac{3}{2}}s}\right).

(3.79)

This strictly improves the estimate on
\Theta
in (
3.13
) for
s_{0}
large enough.

Step 3.
Closing the estimate for
(b_{1},\mu)
.
Recall that, from (
3.26
), (
3.27
) and the bootstrap assumption (
3.13
), we find

|\mathcal{J}_{1}|\lesssim\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\varepsilon^{2}\rho_{1}{\rm d}y\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\rho_{1}{\rm d}y\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim\frac{C_{0}}{s^{\frac{3}{2}}\log^{\frac{1}{2}}s}.

Then, from (
3.78
), Lemma
3.5
and Lemma
3.7
,

\left|\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}s}\left(\mu-5b_{1}z+\mathcal{J}_{1}\right)\right|\lesssim\frac{1}{s^{2}\log s}+\frac{C_{0}^{2}}{s^{\frac{5}{2}}\log s}.

Integrating the above estimate over
[s,s^{in}]
and then using (
3.9
), we find

\mu(s)=5b_{1}(s)z(s)+O\left(\frac{1}{s\log s}+\frac{C^{2}_{0}}{s^{\frac{3}{2}}\log s}\right).

(3.80)

Next, using again (
3.5
), we have

b_{1}=\frac{\dot{z}}{z}-\frac{\mu}{z}+O\left(\frac{C_{0}}{s^{\frac{3}{2}}\log s}\right).

Based on the above estimate and (
3.80
), we find

\displaystyle b_{1}e^{-z}

\displaystyle=\frac{\dot{z}}{6}z^{-1}e^{-z}+O\left(\frac{1}{s^{3}\log^{3}s}+\frac{C_{0}^{2}}{s^{\frac{7}{2}}\log^{3}s}\right)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{6}\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}s}(z^{-1}e^{-z})+O\left(\frac{1}{s^{3}\log^{3}s}+\frac{C_{0}^{2}}{s^{\frac{7}{2}}\log^{3}s}\right).

It follows directly from Lemma
3.5
and (
3.13
) that

\dot{b}_{1}b_{1}=\frac{\alpha}{6}\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}s}(z^{-1}e^{-z})+O\left(\frac{1}{s^{3}\log^{3}s}+\frac{C_{0}^{2}}{s^{\frac{7}{2}}\log^{3}s}\right).

Integrating the above estimate over
[s,s^{in}]
and then using (
3.9
), we find

\displaystyle b^{2}_{1}(s)=\left(\frac{\alpha}{3}\right)z^{-1}e^{-z}+O\left(\frac{1}{s^{2}\log^{3}s}+\frac{C_{0}}{s^{\frac{5}{2}}\log^{3}s}\right).

(3.81)

Therefore, from (
3.77
) and the bootstrap assumption (
3.13
),

\displaystyle\big|b_{1}(s)-\frac{1}{6s\log s}\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{s\log^{\frac{3}{2}}s}+\frac{C_{0}}{s^{\frac{3}{2}}\log^{2}s},

(3.82)

\displaystyle\big|b_{1}(s)-\left(\frac{\alpha}{3}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}z^{-\frac{1}{2}}e^{-\frac{1}{2}z}\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{s\log^{2}s}+\frac{C_{0}}{s^{\frac{3}{2}}\log^{2}s}.

This strictly improves the estimate on
b_{1}
in (
3.13
) for
s_{0}
large enough.

Combining the above estimate with (
3.80
), we obtain

\mu(s)=\frac{5}{3s}+O\left(\frac{1}{s\log^{\frac{1}{2}}s}+\frac{C^{2}_{0}}{s^{\frac{3}{2}}\log^{\frac{1}{2}}s}\right).

(3.83)

This strictly improves the estimate on
\mu
in (
3.13
) for
s_{0}
large enough.

Step 4.

Closing the estimate for
\lambda
.
From (
3.82
) and Lemma
3.5
, we have

\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}s}\log\lambda(s)=-\frac{1}{6s\log s}+O\left(\frac{1}{s\log^{\frac{3}{2}}s}+\frac{C^{2}_{0}}{s^{\frac{3}{2}}\log s}\right).

Based on the above estimate and (
3.9
), we find

\log\lambda(s)=-\frac{1}{6}\log\log s+O\left(\frac{1}{\log^{\frac{1}{2}}s}+\frac{C^{2}_{0}}{s^{\frac{1}{2}}\log s}\right),

which directly implies that

\lambda(s)=\frac{1}{\log^{\frac{1}{6}}s}+O\left(\frac{1}{\log^{\frac{2}{3}}s}+\frac{C^{2}_{0}}{s^{\frac{1}{2}}\log s}\right).

(3.84)

This strictly improves the estimate on
\lambda
in (
3.13
) for
s_{0}
large enough.

Step 5.
Estimate for
(z,x_{1})
.
We need to adjust the final choice of
(z^{in},x_{1}^{in})
via a topological argument (see
[
37
, Section 3.4]
for a similar argument). For any
(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})\in\mathcal{B}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(1)
,
we consider the following choice of finial data

\displaystyle x_{1}^{in}-\frac{s^{in}}{\log^{\frac{1}{6}}s^{in}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\theta_{1}s^{in}}{\log^{\frac{1}{2}}s^{in}},

(3.85)

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{3\alpha}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}(z^{in})^{-\frac{1}{2}}e^{\frac{1}{2}z^{in}}-s^{in}

\displaystyle=\frac{\theta_{2}s^{in}}{\log^{\frac{1}{2}}s^{in}}.

We define
(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})
the following two functions on
[s^{*},s^{in}]
:

\xi_{1}(s)=x_{1}\log^{\frac{1}{6}}s\quad\mbox{and}\quad\xi_{2}(s)=\left(\frac{1}{3\alpha}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}z^{-\frac{1}{2}}e^{\frac{1}{2}z}.

In addition, we denote

\eta(s)=\eta_{1}^{2}(s)+\eta_{2}^{2}(s),\quad\mbox{on}\ [s^{*},s^{in}],

where

\displaystyle\eta_{1}(s)

\displaystyle=(\xi_{1}(s)-s)s^{-1}\log^{\frac{1}{3}}s,\quad\mbox{on}\ [s^{*},s^{in}],

\displaystyle\eta_{2}(s)

\displaystyle=(\xi_{2}(s)-s)s^{-1}\log^{\frac{1}{2}}s,\quad\mbox{on}\ [s^{*},s^{in}].

We claim that, for any
s\in[s^{*},s^{in}]
such that
\eta(s)=1
, the following transversality property holds,

\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}s}\eta(s)\leq-s^{-1}.

(3.86)

Indeed, from (
3.84
) and Lemma
3.5
, we check that

\dot{\xi}_{1}(s)=1+O\left(\frac{1}{\log^{\frac{1}{2}}s}+\frac{C_{0}^{2}}{s^{\frac{1}{2}}\log^{\frac{1}{2}}s}\right).

It follows directly from the bootstrap assumption (
3.13
) that

\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}s}\left(\eta^{2}_{1}(s)\right)=-2s^{-1}\eta_{1}^{2}(s)+O\left(\frac{1}{s\log^{\frac{1}{6}}s}+\frac{C_{0}^{2}}{s^{\frac{3}{2}}\log^{\frac{1}{6}}s}\right).

(3.87)

On the other hand, from (
3.80
), (
3.82
) and Lemma
3.5
, we check that

\dot{z}=6\left(\frac{\alpha}{3}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}z^{\frac{1}{2}}e^{-\frac{1}{2}z}+O\left(\frac{1}{s\log s}+\frac{C^{2}_{0}}{s^{\frac{3}{2}}}+\frac{C^{2}_{0}}{s^{\frac{3}{2}}\log^{\frac{1}{2}}s}\right).

Based on the above estimate and (
3.77
), we find

\dot{\xi}_{2}=1+O\left(\frac{1}{\log s}+\frac{C^{2}_{0}}{s^{\frac{1}{2}}}+\frac{C^{2}_{0}}{s^{\frac{1}{2}}\log^{\frac{1}{2}}s}\right).

It follows from the definition of
\eta_{2}
and (
3.77
) that

\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}s}(\eta_{2}^{2}(s))=-2s^{-1}\eta_{2}^{2}(s)+O\left(\frac{1}{s\log^{\frac{1}{2}}s}+\frac{C_{0}^{2}}{s^{\frac{3}{2}}}\log s\right).

(3.88)

Combining (
3.87
) and (
3.88
), we complete the proof of (
3.86
) for
s_{0}
large enough. The transversality relation (
3.86
) is enough to justify the existence of at least a couple of
(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})\in\mathcal{B}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(1)
such that
s^{*}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})=s_{0}
.

The proof is by contradiction, we assume that for all
(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})\in\mathcal{B}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(1)
, it holds
s_{0}<s^{*}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})
. Then, a contradiction follows from the following discussion (see for example
[
37
, Page 730]
for more discussion).

Continuity of
s^{*}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})
.

The above transversality relation (
3.86
) implies that the map

(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})\in\bar{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(1)\mapsto s^{*}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2}),

is continuous and

s^{*}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})=s^{in},\quad\mbox{for any}\ (\theta_{1},\theta_{2})\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(1).

Construction of a retraction.
We define

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}:(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})\in\mathcal{B}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(1)\mapsto\left(\eta_{1}(s^{*}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})),\eta_{2}(s^{*}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2}))\right)\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(1).

From what precedes,
\mathcal{M}
is continuous. Moreover,
\mathcal{M}
restricted to
\mathcal{S}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(1)
is the identity. The existence of such a map is contradictory to the no retraction theorem for continuous maps from the ball to the sphere. Therefore, the existence of
(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})
have proved and the uniform estimate (
3.10
) is a consequence of the bootstrap estimate (
3.13
), (
3.79
),
(
3.82
), (
3.83
),
(
3.84
) and Lemma
3.3
. The proof of Proposition
3.2
is complete.
∎

4.
Compactness argument

The objective of this section is to finish the construction of Theorem
1.1
by passing to the limit on a sequence of solutions give by Proposition
3.2
.

Proof of Theorem
1.1
.

Step 1.
Construction of a sequence of backwards solutions.
We claim that, there exists
T_{0}>1
and a sequence of solution
u_{n}\in C([T_{0},T_{n}];H^{1})
of (
1.1
) where
T_{n}=n\in\mathbb{N}
satisfying the following estimates on
[T_{0},T_{n}]
,

\left\{\begin{aligned} |z_{n}(t)-2\log t|&\lesssim\log\log t,\quad|b_{1n}(t)|\lesssim\frac{1}{t\log^{\frac{3}{2}}t},\\
|x_{1n}(t)-t\log^{\frac{1}{3}}t|&\lesssim t\log^{\frac{1}{6}}t,\quad\|\varepsilon_{n}(t)\|_{H^{1}}\lesssim\frac{1}{t\log^{\frac{1}{2}}t},\\
\bigg|\lambda_{n}(t)-\frac{1}{\log^{\frac{1}{6}}t}\bigg|&\lesssim\frac{1}{\log^{\frac{2}{3}}t},\quad|\mu_{n}(t)|\lesssim\frac{1}{t\log^{\frac{1}{2}}t},\quad|b_{2n}(t)|\lesssim\frac{1}{t\log^{\frac{3}{2}}t}.\end{aligned}\right.

(4.1)

Here,
(\lambda,z,\mu,x_{1},b_{1},b_{2})\in(0,\infty)^{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{4}
are the parameters for the decomposition of
u_{n}
given by Proposition
3.1
, that is,

\displaystyle u_{n}(t,x)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}_{n}}V\left(\frac{x-x_{1n}}{\lambda_{n}};(z_{n},\mu_{n},b_{1n},b_{2n})\right)+\frac{1}{\lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}_{n}}\varepsilon_{n}\left(t,\frac{x-x_{1n}}{\lambda_{n}}\right)

(4.2)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\lambda_{n}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\left(Q\left(\frac{x-x_{1n}}{\lambda_{n}}\right)-Q\left(\frac{x-x_{1n}}{\lambda_{n}}-z_{n}\right)\right)+O_{H^{1}}\left(\frac{1}{t^{\frac{1}{4}}}\right).

From Proposition
3.2
, we know that there exists a solution
u_{n}(s)
of (
1.1
) on the time interval
[s^{0},s^{in}]
and satisfies the uniform estimate (
3.10
) over such time interval. Recall that,
from (
3.6
)
we set

s(t)=s^{in}-\int_{t}^{T_{n}}\frac{{\rm d}\sigma}{\lambda^{3}(\sigma)}\Longleftrightarrow t(s)=T_{n}-\int_{s}^{s^{in}}\lambda^{3}(\sigma){\rm d}\sigma.

(4.3)

Denote

\eta_{3}(s)=(\xi_{3}(s)-s)^{2}s^{-2}\log^{\frac{1}{2}}s,\quad\mbox{with}\ \ \xi_{3}(s)=t(s)\log^{\frac{1}{2}}s.

From (
3.10
) and (
4.3
), we check that

\eta_{3}(s)=1\Longrightarrow\frac{{\rm d}\eta_{3}}{{\rm d}s}\leq-2s^{-1}+O\left(\frac{1}{s\log^{\frac{1}{4}}s}\right)\leq-s^{-1}.

Therefore, using a similar topological argument as in the step 5 of the proof for Proposition
3.2
, we know that for any
T_{n}=n
large enough, there exists a choice of
s^{in}>0
satisfying (
3.8
) such that

\eta_{3}(s)\leq 1\ \ \mbox{on}\ [s^{*},s^{in}]\Longrightarrow\left|t(s)-\frac{s}{\log^{\frac{1}{2}}s}\right|\leq\frac{s}{\log^{\frac{3}{4}}s},\ \ \mbox{on}\ [s^{*},s^{in}].

(4.4)

It follows directly that

s(t)=t\log^{\frac{1}{2}}t\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{\log^{\frac{1}{4}}t}\right)\right),\quad\mbox{on}\ [T_{0},T_{n}].

(4.5)

Here,
T_{0}>0
is a universal constant independent with the choice of
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Therefore, the estimates (
4.1
) and (
4.2
) are consequence of Proposition
3.2
.

Step 2.
Compactness argument. By (
4.1
) and (
4.2
), the sequence
(u_{n}(T_{0}{}))_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
is bounded in
H^{1}
. Therefore, there exists a subsequence of
(u_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
(still denote by
(u_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
) and
u_{0}\in H^{1}
such that

u_{n}(T_{0})\rightharpoonup u_{0}
weakly in
H^{1}
. Let

u(t)
be the solution of (
1.1
) corresponding to the initial data
u_{0}
. From the local Cauchy theory for (
1.1
) and (
4.1
), we know that
u\in C([T_{0},\infty);H^{1})
and
u(t)
satisfies (
3.2
) for all
t\in[T_{0},\infty)
. Moreover, the decomposition
(\mathcal{G},\varepsilon)
of
u
satisfies, for all
t\in[T_{0},\infty)
,

\mathcal{G}_{n}(t)\to\mathcal{G}(t)\quad\mbox{and}\quad\varepsilon_{n}(t)\rightharpoonup\varepsilon(t)\ \ \mbox{weakly in}\ H^{1}.

In particular, for
t\in[T_{0},\infty)
, the solution
u(t)
decompose as

u(t,x)=\frac{1}{\lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}}V\left(\frac{x-x_{1}}{\lambda};(z,\mu,b_{1},b_{2})\right)+\frac{1}{\lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}}\varepsilon\left(t,\frac{x-x_{1}}{\lambda}\right).

(4.6)

In addition, the function
(\mathcal{G},\varepsilon)
satisfy

\left\{\begin{aligned} |z(t)-2\log t|&\lesssim\log\log t,\quad|b_{1}(t)|\lesssim\frac{1}{t\log^{\frac{3}{2}}t},\\
|x_{1}(t)-t\log^{\frac{1}{3}}t|&\lesssim t\log^{\frac{1}{6}}t,\quad\|\varepsilon(t)\|_{H^{1}}\lesssim\frac{1}{t\log^{\frac{1}{2}}t},\\
\bigg|\lambda(t)-\frac{1}{\log^{\frac{1}{6}}t}\bigg|&\lesssim\frac{1}{\log^{\frac{2}{3}}t},\quad|\mu(t)|\lesssim\frac{1}{t\log^{\frac{1}{2}}t},\quad|b_{2}(t)|\lesssim\frac{1}{t\log^{\frac{3}{2}}t}.\end{aligned}\right.

(4.7)

It follows directly that

\lambda^{-1}(t)\|V(t,y)-Q+Q(\cdot-z)\|_{H^{1}}\lesssim t^{-\frac{1}{4}}.

Therefore, we obtain the following expansion of
u
:

\displaystyle u(t,x)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}(t)}Q\left(\frac{x-x_{1}(t)}{\lambda(t)}\right)+\frac{1}{\lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}(t)}\varepsilon\left(t,\frac{x-x_{1}(t)}{\lambda(t)}\right)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}(t)}Q\left(\frac{x-x_{1}(t)-\lambda(t)z(t)}{\lambda(t)}\right)+O(t^{-\frac{1}{4}}).

Combining above estimate with (
4.7
), we complete the proof for Theorem
1.1
.
∎
